@echo off
REM Best-results training pipeline (unattended, laptop CPU). Order matters:
REM   1. wait for keypoint extraction (ISLRTC reference + new-signer clips) and pretraining downloads
REM   2. pretraining cache (NIOS, Sign Library, ISH News, ISLRTC non-held-out words)
REM   3. self-supervised pretraining: two encoder sizes
REM   4. Optuna search (30 trials, median pruning) on INCLUDE-val + ISLRTC-val (different signers)
REM   5. final 5-seed ensemble on INCLUDE train+val, temperature calibration, ONNX export
REM   6. one evaluation on INCLUDE official test + ISLRTC-test
REM Progress: models\best\train_best.log  ;  stage markers: models\best\stage_*.done
cd /d E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor
set PY=E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\.venv\Scripts\python.exe
set TORCH_THREADS=12
if not exist models\best mkdir models\best

:wait_extract
if not exist data\processed\extract_done.flag ( timeout /t 120 /nobreak > nul & goto wait_extract )
:wait_dl
findstr /c:"ALL DOWNLOADS COMPLETE" data\manifests\supervisor.log > nul || ( timeout /t 300 /nobreak > nul & goto wait_dl )

if not exist models\best\stage_cache.done (
  "%PY%" -u scripts\build_pretrain_cache.py all >> models\best\pipeline.log 2>&1 && echo done > models\best\stage_cache.done
)
if not exist models\best\stage_pretrain192.done (
  "%PY%" -u scripts\train_best.py pretrain --pt-d 192 --pt-depth 2 --pt-steps 20000 >> models\best\pipeline.log 2>&1 && echo done > models\best\stage_pretrain192.done
)
if not exist models\best\stage_pretrain256.done (
  "%PY%" -u scripts\train_best.py pretrain --pt-d 256 --pt-depth 2 --pt-steps 20000 >> models\best\pipeline.log 2>&1 && echo done > models\best\stage_pretrain256.done
)
if not exist models\best\stage_hpo.done (
  "%PY%" -u scripts\train_best.py hpo --trials 30 >> models\best\pipeline.log 2>&1 && echo done > models\best\stage_hpo.done
)
if not exist models\best\stage_final.done (
  "%PY%" -u scripts\train_best.py final --seeds 5 >> models\best\pipeline.log 2>&1 && echo done > models\best\stage_final.done
)
if not exist models\best\stage_eval.done (
  "%PY%" -u scripts\train_best.py eval >> models\best\pipeline.log 2>&1 && echo done > models\best\stage_eval.done
)
echo PIPELINE FINISHED > models\best\PIPELINE_FINISHED.flag

Write: E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\isl_tutor\runtime.py
input
"""Live inference pieces shared by the web demo (app/server.py) and the CLI (app/live_tutor.py).

Tracker      MediaPipe Holistic (same model/fps as training extraction) -> per-frame arrays
Recognizer   models/recognizer.onnx + recognizer_meta.json (ensemble, temperature-calibrated)
LiveSegmenter  turns a frame stream into finished sign attempts (rest -> sign -> rest)
"""
import importlib.util, json, os, time
from collections import deque

import numpy as np

from .data import featurise
from .features import sign_segments

ROOT = os.path.abspath(os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), ".."))
MODEL_TASK = os.path.join(ROOT, "models", "mediapipe", "holistic_landmarker.task")
REC_ONNX = os.path.join(ROOT, "models", "recognizer.onnx")
REC_META = os.path.join(ROOT, "models", "recognizer_meta.json")
FPS = 12

# the face subset must match extraction exactly -> take it from the extractor itself
_spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("_extract", os.path.join(ROOT, "scripts", "extract_keypoints.py"))
_ext = importlib.util.module_from_spec(_spec)
_spec.loader.exec_module(_ext)
FACE_IDX = _ext.FACE_IDX


class Tracker:
    def __init__(self):
        import mediapipe as mp
        from mediapipe.tasks.python import vision, BaseOptions
        self.mp = mp
        self.det = vision.HolisticLandmarker.create_from_options(vision.HolisticLandmarkerOptions(
            base_options=BaseOptions(model_asset_path=MODEL_TASK), running_mode=vision.RunningMode.VIDEO))
        self.last_ts = -1

    def __call__(self, rgb, ts_ms):
        ts = max(int(ts_ms), self.last_ts + 1)
        self.last_ts = ts
        r = self.det.detect_for_video(self.mp.Image(image_format=self.mp.ImageFormat.SRGB, data=rgb), ts)

        def arr(lms, n, k):
            if not lms:
                return np.full((n, k), np.nan, np.float32)
            return np.array([[p.x, p.y, p.z] + ([p.visibility or 0.0] if k == 4 else []) for p in lms], np.float32)
        face = arr(r.face_landmarks, 478, 3)[FACE_IDX]
        return arr(r.pose_landmarks, 33, 4), arr(r.left_hand_landmarks, 21, 3), arr(r.right_hand_landmarks, 21, 3), face

    def close(self):
        self.det.close()


class Recognizer:
    def __init__(self, onnx_path=REC_ONNX, meta_path=REC_META):
        import onnxruntime as ort
        so = ort.SessionOptions()
        so.intra_op_num_threads = 2
        self.sess = ort.InferenceSession(onnx_path, so, providers=["CPUExecutionProvider"])
        with open(meta_path) as fh:
            m = json.load(fh)

"""ISL Sign Tutor - local demo server.

Run:   .venv\\Scripts\\python app\\server.py      then open  http://localhost:8000
The browser streams webcam frames (JPEG, ~12 fps) over a WebSocket; this server runs the SAME
MediaPipe + recogniser + checker code used in training and evaluation, and sends back landmarks,
recognitions and corrections.
"""
import asyncio, base64, glob, json, os, re, sys, time

import cv2
import numpy as np
from fastapi import FastAPI, WebSocket, WebSocketDisconnect
from fastapi.responses import FileResponse, JSONResponse
from fastapi.staticfiles import StaticFiles

ROOT = os.path.abspath(os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), ".."))
sys.path.insert(0, ROOT)
from isl_tutor.checker import compare                       # noqa: E402
from isl_tutor.gloss import to_gloss                       # noqa: E402
from isl_tutor.runtime import LiveSegmenter, Recognizer, Tracker  # noqa: E402
from isl_tutor.sequence import ATTRIB, _norm, load_index, plan, render  # noqa: E402

WEB = os.path.join(ROOT, "app", "web")
CACHE = os.path.join(ROOT, "outputs", "web")
os.makedirs(CACHE, exist_ok=True)
PROC = os.path.join(ROOT, "data", "processed")
app = FastAPI(title="ISL Sign Tutor")
app.mount("/static", StaticFiles(directory=WEB), name="static")
app.mount("/media", StaticFiles(directory=CACHE), name="media")

INDEX = load_index()
REC = Recognizer() if Recognizer.available() else None


def _key(w):
    return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]", "", w.lower())


def vocabulary():
    """Teachable words = recogniser classes that also have an ISLRTC reference clip."""
    words = INDEX["words"]
    lookup = {_key(k): k for k in words}
    out = []
    for c in (REC.classes if REC else []):
        k = lookup.get(_key(c))
        if not k:
            continue
        clip = words[k][0]
        npz = os.path.join(PROC, "islrtc", f"{os.path.basename(os.path.dirname(clip))}__{os.path.basename(clip)[:-4]}.npz")
        out.append({"word": c, "label": k, "clip": clip, "ref_npz": npz if os.path.exists(npz) else None})
    return out


VOCAB = {v["word"]: v for v in vocabulary()}


@app.get("/")
def home():
    return FileResponse(os.path.join(WEB, "index.html"))

"""Gloss sequence -> English: phrasebook first, then the local LLM (Ollama, llama3.2), then raw glosses."""
import json, urllib.request

PHRASEBOOK = {"YOUR NAME WHAT": "What is your name?", "BANK WHERE": "Where is the bank?",
              "I FINE": "I am fine.", "THANK YOU": "Thank you.", "GOOD MORNING": "Good morning."}
PROMPT = ("You convert Indian Sign Language glosses into one natural English sentence. ISL order: time first, "
          "verb last, question word and NOT at the end; no articles or tense. Reply with the sentence only.\n"
          "Glosses: TOMORROW I SCHOOL GO -> I will go to school tomorrow.\n"
          "Glosses: SHE APPLE RED LIKE NOT -> She does not like red apples.\n"
          "Glosses: {g} ->")


def gloss_to_english(glosses, model="llama3.2"):
    key = " ".join(glosses).upper()
    if key in PHRASEBOOK:
        return PHRASEBOOK[key]
    try:
        req = urllib.request.Request("http://localhost:11434/api/generate", data=json.dumps(
            {"model": model, "prompt": PROMPT.format(g=key), "stream": False,
             "options": {"temperature": 0, "num_predict": 40}}).encode(), headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
        out = json.load(urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30))["response"].strip().split("\n")[0]
        return out or key.capitalize()
    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - Ollama not running
        return key.capitalize()
output

Now the page's JavaScript: the tabs, the webcam stream with back-pressure (only one frame in flight at a time, so it never builds a lag queue), the hand overlay, and the rendering of results.

Write: E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\app\web\app.js
input
// ISL Sign Tutor - front end. Webcam frames -> WebSocket (backend runs MediaPipe + model) -> overlay + results.
const $ = (s) => document.querySelector(s);
let META = null, target = null;

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- tabs
document.querySelectorAll("#tabs button").forEach((b) => b.onclick = () => {
  document.querySelectorAll("#tabs button").forEach((x) => x.classList.toggle("on", x === b));
  document.querySelectorAll(".tab").forEach((t) => t.classList.toggle("on", t.id === b.dataset.tab));
});

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- meta / overview
const pct = (x) => (x == null ? "—" : (100 * x).toFixed(1) + "%");
function evalRow(key) {
  if (!META || !META.eval) return null;
  return META.eval[key] || null;
}
async function loadMeta() {
  META = await (await fetch("/api/meta")).json();
  const st = $("#status");
  st.textContent = META.model_ready ? `model ready · ${META.classes} signs · ${META.vocabulary.length} with reference videos`
                                    : "model is still training — Show me works already";
  st.classList.toggle("warn", !META.model_ready);
  const inc = evalRow("recognizer.onnx | INCLUDE official test");
  const isl = evalRow("recognizer.onnx | ISLRTC-test (new signers, held-out words)");
  const lat = META.eval ? META.eval["recognizer.onnx | latency_ms"] : null;
  const size = META.eval ? META.eval["recognizer.onnx | size_mb"] : null;
  $("#metrics").innerHTML = [
    ["INCLUDE test top-1", inc ? pct(inc.top1) : "training…"],
    ["New signers top-5", isl ? pct(isl.top5) : "training…"],
    ["Recognition time", lat ? lat.toFixed(1) + " ms" : "~1–4 ms"],
    ["Model size", size ? size.toFixed(1) + " MB" : "a few MB"],
  ].map(([k, v]) => `<div class="metric"><div class="v">${v}</div><div class="k">${k}</div></div>`).join("");
  const ours = isl ? isl.top1 : null;
  const rows = [["GPT-5 (zero-shot, ASL)", 0.1557], ["Gemini 2.5 Pro (zero-shot, ASL)", 0.2365],
                ["Specialised model (ASL, published)", 0.8997]];
  if (ours != null) rows.push(["This tutor (ISL, new signers)", ours]);
  $("#vsbars").innerHTML = rows.map(([k, v], i) =>
    `<div class="bar ${i === 3 ? "ours" : ""}"><span>${k}</span><div class="track"><div class="fill" style="width:${(100 * v).toFixed(1)}%"></div></div><span>${pct(v)}</span></div>`).join("");
  $("#footer").textContent = META.attribution + " · INCLUDE: AI4Bharat (CC-BY-4.0)";
  renderWords("");
  renderResults();
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- practice: vocabulary
function renderWords(q) {
  const list = META.vocabulary.filter((v) => v.label.toLowerCase().includes(q.toLowerCase()));
  $("#vocabCount").textContent = `${list.length} words`;
  $("#wordList").innerHTML = list.slice(0, 400).map((v) =>
    `<span class="word ${v.word === target ? "on" : ""} ${v.can_correct ? "" : "nocorr"}" data-w="${v.word}" title="${v.can_correct ? "" : "recognition only"}">${v.label}</span>`).join("");

import asyncio, base64, json, time, zipfile, tempfile, os, cv2, websockets
z = zipfile.ZipFile("data/raw/include_videos/Greetings_1of2.zip"); m = [n for n in z.namelist() if n.lower().endswith(".mov")][0]
fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(suffix=".mov"); os.write(fd, z.read(m)); os.close(fd)
async def main():
    async with websockets.connect("ws://localhost:8765/ws?mode=practice&target=") as ws:
        cap = cv2.VideoCapture(tmp); fps = cap.get(5); i = n = 0; times = []; got = 0
        while True:
            ok, fr = cap.read()
            if not ok: break
            if i % max(1, round(fps/12)) == 0:
                fr = cv2.resize(fr, (640, 480)); jpg = base64.b64encode(cv2.imencode(".jpg", fr, [cv2.IMWRITE_JPEG_QUALITY, 70])[1]).decode()
                t = time.perf_counter(); await ws.send(json.dumps({"type": "frame", "ts": int(i*1000/fps), "jpg": jpg}))
                r = json.loads(await ws.recv()); times.append((time.perf_counter()-t)*1000); n += 1
                got += bool(r.get("lh") or r.get("rh"))
            i += 1
        print(f"{m}: {n} frames sent, round-trip mean {sum(times)/n:.0f} ms (p90 {sorted(times)[int(.9*n)]:.0f} ms), hands in {got}/{n} frames")
asyncio.run(main()); os.remove(tmp)

I've reorganised everything around the best result, not the fastest, and built the demo webpage. Training hasn't started yet. It waits until all the data is ready, then runs by itself.

Order of work (all on your laptop CPU, unattended):

# Step Status Time (estimate)
1 Extract the single-word ISLRTC reference clips (4,389 of them) running, 1,000 done about 1.5 h
2 Pretraining cache: NIOS (138 h) and Sign Library (6.4 h) are built; ISH News and the ISLRTC clips still to add partly done about 30 min
3 Self-supervised pretraining: the model learns ISL motion from about 150 h of unlabelled signing, for two model sizes queued about 6–9 h
4 Optuna search: 30 trials over model size, augmentation, regularisation and use of facial cues, with weak trials stopped early queued about 1 day
5 Final model: best settings × 5 seeds, ensembled and calibrated, exported for the CPU queued about 6–10 h
6 One final test on INCLUDE's test set and on ISLRTC signers the model has never seen queued minutes

That's roughly 1.5–2 days in total. The pipeline (scripts/run_training_pipeline.cmd) resumes from its last finished step if it's interrupted. To watch progress, open models\best\train_best.log.

What I changed to protect result quality:

  • Fixed a test-set bug: INCLUDE reuses camera file names (MVI_xxxx) across categories, so matching on file name alone pulled 977 clips into the test set. Matching on category + word + file now gives the correct 816.
  • New-signer validation for tuning: the 193 words shared between INCLUDE and ISLRTC are split by word. 60 words are used for tuning and 133 only for the final test. All of them are kept out of pretraining, even unlabelled.
  • Every stage tested end to end on a short smoke run: the exported model is 1.7 MB and recognises a sign in 1.2 ms.

The demo webpage: double-click E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\run_demo.cmd and allow camera access. It opens at http://localhost:8765. Port 8000 is already used by another app/server.py of yours (probably the OCR demo), so I left that alone. It has five tabs:

  • Overview: headline results, plus a bar chart against GPT-5 and Gemini.
  • Practice: pick a word, watch the ISLRTC reference (with a slow-motion button), sign to your webcam with a live hand overlay, then get the verdict, the top-3 guesses, the fix, and a per-parameter breakdown.
  • Translate: live sign chips, then the English sentence from the local LLM.
  • Show me: type a sentence and see the ISL word order and the sign videos in sequence.
  • Results: the full evaluation table and the chosen configuration.

What I've tested so far: "Show me" built "Where is the bank?" → BANK WHERE in 4 seconds. The live stream tracked hands in 32 of 32 frames at about 58 ms per frame, within the 83 ms that 12 fps allows. Practice and Translate show results only once the model exists. I'll test them end to end, take screenshots, and fill in the real numbers when the training finishes.

Two things only you can do:

  2. Optional but valuable: add kaggle.json or run hf auth login. More signers is the biggest remaining accuracy lever. If you add either before step 4 starts (in about 8–10 h), I'll add those datasets to the training.

Write: E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\kaggle\isl_train.py
input
"""ISL recogniser - best-results training on Kaggle GPU (T4 x2).

Stages:  pretrain  -> hpo -> final -> eval        (see KAGGLE.md for the cells)
  pretrain  masked-frame reconstruction on ~150 h of unlabelled ISL pose (NIOS, Sign Library, ISH News)
  hpo       Optuna TPE + median pruning; objective = mean(INCLUDE-val top-1, ISLRTC-val top-1 [new signers])
  final     best config, 5 seeds on INCLUDE train+val, weight EMA, ensemble, temperature calibration, ONNX export
  eval      one evaluation: INCLUDE official test + ISLRTC-test (new signers, words never used for tuning)

Data hygiene: the INCLUDE official test and ISLRTC-test are touched ONLY by `eval`.
Train-time augmentation runs on the GPU; eval/export features use isl_tutor/data.py (identical to the app).
"""
import argparse, copy, glob, json, math, os, pickle, random, re, sys, time
import numpy as np
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

DATA = os.environ.get("ISL_DATA", "/kaggle/input/isl-sign-tutor-data")
OUT = os.environ.get("ISL_OUT", "/kaggle/working/isl")
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(DATA, "code"))
from isl_tutor.data import FLIP, NM, P, features, resample  # noqa: E402  (same code as the app)

os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
LOG = os.path.join(OUT, "train.log")


def log(*a):
    s = " ".join(str(x) for x in a)
    print(s, flush=True)
    with open(LOG, "a") as fh:
        fh.write(time.strftime("%H:%M:%S ") + s + "\n")


def seed_all(s):
    random.seed(s); np.random.seed(s); torch.manual_seed(s); torch.cuda.manual_seed_all(s)


# ===================================================================================== data
def load_labelled():
    with open(os.path.join(DATA, "labelled.pkl"), "rb") as fh:
        d = pickle.load(fh)
    return d["classes"], d["include"], d["islrtc"]


def split_train_val(inc, seed=0):
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    byw = {}
    for it in inc:
        if it["split"] == "train":
            byw.setdefault(it["word"], []).append(it)
    tr, va = [], []
    for w in sorted(byw):
        v = byw[w][:]
        rng.shuffle(v)
        k = max(1, round(0.15 * len(v)))
        va += v[:k]; tr += v[k:]
    return tr, va

Write: E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\scripts\pack_kaggle_dataset.py
input
"""Assemble the Kaggle dataset folder: kaggle/dataset/  (upload this folder as the dataset 'isl-sign-tutor-data').

Contents
  labelled.pkl        INCLUDE clips (split train/test) + ISLRTC new-signer clips (split val/test), already
                      normalised with isl_tutor.data (body-frame points float32 + non-manual features)
  pretrain/*.npy      unlabelled ISL pose banks (NIOS, Sign Library, ISH News[, ISLRTC non-held-out words])
  code/               isl_tutor/{data,features}.py (identical to the app) + isl_train.py
  README.md, dataset-metadata.json
Usage: python scripts/pack_kaggle_dataset.py [--no-pretrain]
"""
import argparse, json, os, pickle, shutil, sys

ROOT = os.path.abspath(os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), ".."))
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(ROOT, "scripts"))
sys.argv, _argv = sys.argv[:1], sys.argv          # train_best parses no args at import, but be safe
import train_best as tb                           # noqa: E402  (load_labelled has the split fixes)
sys.argv = _argv

OUT = os.path.join(ROOT, "kaggle", "dataset")


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--no-pretrain", action="store_true")
    a = ap.parse_args()
    os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
    classes, inc, isl = tb.load_labelled()
    inc_out = [dict(pts=c["pts"], nm=c["nm"], word=c["word"], vid=c["vid"], split="test" if c["test"] else "train")
               for c in inc]
    isl_out = [dict(pts=c["pts"], nm=c["nm"], word=c["word"], split=c["split"]) for c in isl]
    with open(os.path.join(OUT, "labelled.pkl"), "wb") as fh:
        pickle.dump({"classes": classes, "include": inc_out, "islrtc": isl_out}, fh, protocol=4)
    stats = {"classes": len(classes),
             "include_train": sum(c["split"] == "train" for c in inc_out),
             "include_test": sum(c["split"] == "test" for c in inc_out),
             "islrtc_val_clips": sum(c["split"] == "val" for c in isl_out),
             "islrtc_test_clips": sum(c["split"] == "test" for c in isl_out),
             "islrtc_val_words": len({c["word"] for c in isl_out if c["split"] == "val"}),
             "islrtc_test_words": len({c["word"] for c in isl_out if c["split"] == "test"})}
    print("labelled:", stats)
    code = os.path.join(OUT, "code", "isl_tutor")
    os.makedirs(code, exist_ok=True)
    open(os.path.join(code, "__init__.py"), "w").close()
    for f in ("data.py", "features.py"):
        shutil.copy2(os.path.join(ROOT, "isl_tutor", f), os.path.join(code, f))
    shutil.copy2(os.path.join(ROOT, "kaggle", "isl_train.py"), os.path.join(OUT, "code", "isl_train.py"))
    pre_stats = {}
    if not a.no_pretrain:
        dst = os.path.join(OUT, "pretrain")
        os.makedirs(dst, exist_ok=True)
        src = os.path.join(ROOT, "data", "processed", "pretrain")
        for f in sorted(os.listdir(src)):
            if f.endswith(".npy"):
                s, d = os.path.join(src, f), os.path.join(dst, f)
                if not (os.path.exists(d) and os.path.getsize(d) == os.path.getsize(s)):
                    shutil.copy2(s, d)
                pre_stats[f] = round(os.path.getsize(d) / 1e6, 1)

# Training the ISL recogniser on Kaggle (T4 × 2)

The best-results recipe moves from the laptop (about 1.5–2 days on CPU) to a free Kaggle GPU session (estimated **2–4 hours**). Everything except running the cells is already prepared.

| | |
|---|---|
| What you upload | `E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\kaggle\dataset\` (keypoints only, no videos, about 2.4 GB) |
| What runs on Kaggle | self-supervised pretraining → Optuna search (60 trials) → 5-seed ensemble → one evaluation |
| What you download | `isl_outputs.zip` (the model is a few MB) → unzip into `models\` → run `run_demo.cmd` |

> The model is the same one the local demo runs. `code/isl_tutor/data.py` in the dataset is a copy of the app's own normalisation code, so the accuracy measured on Kaggle carries over to your laptop.

---

## 1. Upload the dataset (once)

**Option A — browser (no token needed)**
1. Go to kaggle.com → **Datasets** → **+ New Dataset**.
2. Drag in **the contents of** `kaggle\dataset\`, keeping the folder structure:
   ```
   labelled.pkl
   README.md
   code/isl_train.py
   code/isl_tutor/__init__.py, data.py, features.py
   pretrain/oh_nios.npy, oh_nios_index.npy, oh_sign_library.npy, oh_sign_library_index.npy,
            oh_ish_news.npy, oh_ish_news_index.npy
   ```
   Skip `dataset-metadata.json`; it is only for option B.
3. Title: **`isl-sign-tutor-data`**. Kaggle turns this into the URL slug, and the notebook expects that exact slug. Keep it **Private**. Press **Create**.
4. Wait until the upload says *"Your dataset is ready"*. The NIOS file is 1.7 GB.

**Option B — command line** (if you put `kaggle.json` in `C:\Users\adity\.kaggle\`)
1. Edit `kaggle\dataset\dataset-metadata.json` and replace `YOUR_KAGGLE_USERNAME`.
2. Run:
   ```
   pip install kaggle
   kaggle datasets create -p E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\kaggle\dataset --dir-mode skip
   ```

If you upload under a different name, change `ISL_DATA` in cell 1 to the folder Kaggle shows under `/kaggle/input/`.

## 2. Create the notebook

**Code → New Notebook**, then in the right-hand panel:

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Accelerator | **GPU T4 × 2** |
| Internet | **On** (for `pip install optuna onnxruntime`) |
| Add Input | your dataset `isl-sign-tutor-data` |
| Persistence | Files only (optional) |

> **Run it as a background job.** After adding the cells, use **Save Version → Save & Run All (Commit)**. The run then continues with the browser closed, and `/kaggle/working` is kept with the version. Interactive sessions lose their files when they time out.

---

The limit is labelled video per word, not compute. A larger GPU doesn't help when a word has only one example. Here's every large ISL vocabulary source I've checked:

Source Words Videos per word Different signers per word Access
INCLUDE (what we train on) 263 ~16 7 open ✔ downloaded
ISLRTC dictionary 12,617 1 (a few have a 2nd variant) 1 open ✔ downloaded
WSLP 2025 word-recognition task 4,361 1 (4,398 training videos; test labels hidden) 1 open, non-commercial / no-derivatives licence
CISLR ~4,700 ~1.5 ~1 Hugging Face login
FDMSE-ISL (2024 paper) 2,002 20 20 no public download; must email the authors
Kaggle ISL sets 11–60 60–400 4–8 needs your Kaggle token

So only INCLUDE gives many signers per word publicly. Everything bigger is essentially one video per word, because those datasets are built from dictionaries.

What makes a large vocabulary hard:

  1. One example per word. A classifier can't learn a word reliably from one clip. Published one-shot results on large ISL vocabularies: CISLR gets 16.8% top-1 over about 4,700 words, and the WSLP baseline gets 54% top-1 / 78% top-5 over about 4,400 words.
  2. Honest testing becomes impossible. With one signer per word, you can't measure how well it works on a new person for that word.
  3. More look-alikes. Many signs differ only in handshape or location, so confusions grow with the vocabulary.
  4. Messy labels. Dictionary entries include phrases, "Sign 2" variants, regional forms and compounds, which need cleaning.
  5. Local preprocessing. All 13,665 dictionary clips need keypoint extraction on your CPU. The clips of 8 s or shorter (4,389 of them) are already being extracted. For the rest, extracting just the first ~10 s (where the word's sign is) would take roughly a night (my estimate).

The reframe that makes thousands of words possible: in practice and correction mode, the learner has already chosen the word. So the task isn't "which of 12,000 words is this?" but "does this match the DOCTOR reference?", a yes/no comparison. That needs only one good reference per word, which the ISLRTC dictionary provides.

The way to do it is to train a sign-embedding model. It maps any sign to a point in a space where the same word lands close together, whoever signs it. Training uses:

  • INCLUDE's multi-signer examples;
  • the 193 words that appear in both INCLUDE and ISLRTC (same word, different people and cameras), the most valuable pairs we have;
  • augmented copies of the single ISLRTC clips;
  • the 190 hours of pretraining motion.

What each feature would become:

Feature Now With the embedding model
Practice / correct 263 words every single-word ISLRTC entry: about 4,000 now, up to about 12,000 once all clips are extracted
Recognise ("what did I sign?") 263 words, high accuracy about 4,000 words with lower accuracy (top-5 matters more than top-1); the 263 INCLUDE words stay the most accurate
Translate 263 words larger vocabulary, held together by the phrasebook constraint

What I'd do:

  1. Extend the Kaggle script: add the embedding (metric-learning) training next to the classifier, measured honestly. The tests are yes/no accuracy on held-out words with new signers (INCLUDE clips against ISLRTC references for the 133 test words, which is genuinely cross-signer), plus top-k retrieval over the full vocabulary. The extra Kaggle compute is small.
  2. Locally: finish the ≤8 s extraction, then extract the first sign from all remaining dictionary clips overnight, and add those references to the dataset.
  3. Your side (optional, high value):
    • email the FDMSE-ISL authors for the 2,002-word, 20-signer dataset — the biggest possible gain;
    • run hf auth login for CISLR;
    • add a Kaggle token for the multi-signer Kaggle sets.

For the upload: go ahead and upload the current dataset folder now; it stays valid. If you agree, I'll add the embedding training and the large reference set as a new version of the same Kaggle dataset (only new files and the updated isl_train.py). In that case, wait for my updated KAGGLE.md before running the cells.

Should I go ahead with the embedding model?

c = {r["class"] for r in csv.DictReader(open("data_meta/metadata_composite.csv", encoding="utf-8"))}
print("atomic classes", len(a), "| composite classes", len(c))

Words 2,002 (1,099 single signs, 352 compounds, plus others)

The split is properly by signer, so its test score is a genuine "new person" score, which is exactly what we need.

Is Kaggle faster? For training, yes, by a lot. For the first step, no:

Step Where Why
1. Turn videos into keypoints Your laptop Reaching Kaggle would mean uploading 30+ GB of videos, which the usage agreement forbids. Kaggle's 4 CPUs would also be slower than your 16 threads, since MediaPipe runs on CPU either way. My estimate is about 8–10 hours for the 40,033 front-view videos.
2. Train Kaggle (if permitted) The keypoints are only about 0.5–1 GB. 20,016 training clips is 6× INCLUDE, so local CPU training would take about 5 h for a single configuration, with no room for tuning. On Kaggle the full recipe (pretraining + search + 5-seed ensemble) is an estimated 3–5 h.

One condition before uploading anything to Kaggle: the agreement forbids "public hosting… or transfer", and you asked the authors whether a private Kaggle upload of keypoints is allowed. Only upload if they said yes. If they didn't, or haven't replied, the fallback is training locally on your CPU with one good configuration instead of a full search: slower, but within the rules.

The demo also changes because of the agreement. You promised not to show FDMSE videos, so the demo will show references as animated skeletons drawn from the keypoints, not as videos.

What I'm doing now (needed whichever way we train): I'm adding the FDMSE source to the extractor and starting front-view keypoint extraction on your laptop, keeping the official signer splits.

cd /e/AIDS/Project/isl-sign-tutor && /c/Users/adity/AppData/Local/Programs/Python/Python312/python.exe - <<'EOF'
p = "scripts/extract_keypoints.py"; s = open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
add = '''

def fdmse_jobs(view="front"):
    """FDMSE-ISL (data/Raw/FDMSE-ISL): metadata.csv rows (front view, official signer-disjoint splits).
    view='left'/'right' maps the same rows to the side cameras (s0001_f_w000001 -> s0001_l_w000001)."""
    base = os.path.join(ROOT, "data", "Raw", "FDMSE-ISL")
    with open(os.path.join(base, "data_meta", "metadata.csv"), encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        for r in csv.DictReader(fh):
            rel = r["video_dir"]
            if view != "front":
                rel = rel.replace("/front/", f"/{view}/").replace("_f_w", "_" + view[0] + "_w")
            stem = os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(rel))[0]
            yield dict(id=stem, word=r["class"], src=os.path.join(base, rel), zip="", split=r["split"])
'''
s = s.replace("\n\ndef worker(job):", add + "\n\ndef worker(job):", 1)
s = s.replace('ap.add_argument("source", choices=["islrtc", "include"])', 'ap.add_argument("source", choices=["islrtc", "include", "fdmse"])\n    ap.add_argument("--view", default="front", help="fdmse: front|left|right")')
s = s.replace('    out_dir = os.path.join(OUT, a.source)\n', '    out_dir = os.path.join(OUT, a.source if a.source != "fdmse" else f"fdmse_{a.view}")\n')
s = s.replace('jobs = list(islrtc_jobs(a.max_dur, a.words_file) if a.source == "islrtc" else include_jobs())',
              'jobs = list(islrtc_jobs(a.max_dur, a.words_file) if a.source == "islrtc" else\n                fdmse_jobs(a.view) if a.source == "fdmse" else include_jobs())')
s = s.replace('fields = ["id", "word", "frames"', 'fields = ["id", "word", "split", "frames"')
s = s.replace('        return dict(id=job["id"], word=job["word"], frames=T,', '        return dict(id=job["id"], word=job["word"], split=job.get("split", ""), frames=T,')
open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)
EOF

p = "scripts/extract_keypoints.py"; s = open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
old = '''    while True:
        ok, fr = cap.read()
        if not ok:
            break
        if i % step == 0:'''
new = '''    while True:
        if i % step != 0:                                 # skipped frame: advance without converting pixels
            if not cap.grab():
                break
            i += 1
            continue
        ok, fr = cap.read()
        if not ok:
            break
        if i % step == 0:'''
assert old in s; s = s.replace(old, new); open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)

input
"""Assemble the Kaggle dataset folder from FDMSE-ISL keypoints: kaggle/dataset/  (dataset slug 'isl-sign-tutor-data').

Contents (keypoints only - no video, no frames):
  labelled.pkl   {"classes": [2002 words], "sets": {"train"|"val"|"test": [clip dicts]}}
                 clip = {pts (n,67,2) float32 body-frame points, nm (n,5) non-manual, word, signer, id}
                 splits = FDMSE official, signer-disjoint (train 10 signers, val 2, test 8)
  code/          isl_tutor/{data,features}.py (identical to the app) + isl_train.py
  README.md, dataset-metadata.json

Licence note: FDMSE-ISL may not be redistributed or publicly hosted. Upload only as a PRIVATE dataset,
and only if the dataset authors have agreed (see KAGGLE.md).
Usage: python scripts/pack_kaggle_dataset.py [--min-hand-rate 0.3]
"""
import argparse, csv, glob, json, os, pickle, shutil, sys
import numpy as np

ROOT = os.path.abspath(os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), ".."))
sys.path.insert(0, ROOT)
from isl_tutor.data import nonmanual_from_face, points_from_arrays  # noqa: E402

PROC = os.path.join(ROOT, "data", "processed", "fdmse_front")
OUT = os.path.join(ROOT, "kaggle", "dataset")


def load_clip(path):
    d = np.load(path)
    s, e = d["active"]
    pose, lh, rh = (d[k][s:e + 1].astype(np.float32) for k in ("pose", "lh", "rh"))
    face = d["face"][s:e + 1].astype(np.float32)
    pts, _ = points_from_arrays(pose, lh, rh)
    return pts, nonmanual_from_face(face, pose)


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--min-hand-rate", type=float, default=0.3, help="drop clips where hands are almost never seen")
    a = ap.parse_args()
    with open(os.path.join(PROC, "manifest.csv"), encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        rows = [r for r in csv.DictReader(fh) if r["status"] == "ok"]
    seen, keep, dropped = set(), [], 0
    for r in rows:                                   # manifest is append-only: keep the latest row per id
        if r["id"] in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(r["id"])
        if float(r["hand_rate"]) < a.min_hand_rate:
            dropped += 1
            continue
        keep.append(r)
    classes = sorted({r["word"] for r in keep})
    sets = {"train": [], "val": [], "test": []}
    for i, r in enumerate(keep):
        pts, nm = load_clip(os.path.join(PROC, r["id"] + ".npz"))
        sets[r["split"]].append(dict(pts=pts, nm=nm, word=r["word"], signer=r["id"][:5], id=r["id"]))
        if (i + 1) % 5000 == 0:
            print(f"{i+1}/{len(keep)} clips packed", flush=True)
    os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
    with open(os.path.join(OUT, "labelled.pkl"), "wb") as fh:
        pickle.dump({"classes": classes, "sets": sets}, fh, protocol=4)
    signers = {k: sorted({c["signer"] for c in v}) for k, v in sets.items()}
    overlap = set(signers["train"]) & (set(signers["val"]) | set(signers["test"]))
… 35 more lines not shown

# Training the ISL recogniser on Kaggle (FDMSE-ISL, T4 × 2)

| Step | Where | Time |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Videos → keypoints (40,033 front-view clips) | **your laptop** (videos must not leave it) | ~7–8 h, running now |
| 2. Pack the keypoint dataset | your laptop (I do this) | a few minutes |
| 3. Upload as a **private** Kaggle dataset | you, in the browser | ~10 min |
| 4. Search + 5-seed ensemble + evaluation | Kaggle T4 × 2 | an estimated 3–5 h |
| 5. Bring the model home, run the demo | your laptop | minutes |

> ⚠ **Licence gate — read before step 3.** The FDMSE-ISL agreement forbids redistribution, sharing and public hosting. The dataset we upload contains **keypoints only** (no video, no frames), is marked **private**, and is used only by you. **Upload it only if the dataset authors agreed** to this in reply to your email. If they did not, or have not replied, tell me and we will train on the laptop instead: a single tuned configuration, roughly 5–6 h per run on the CPU, no search.

---

## 1. The dataset (I build it when extraction finishes)

`E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\kaggle\dataset\` will contain:
```
labelled.pkl                      keypoints of all clips, official FDMSE splits (est. 0.7 GB)
code/isl_train.py                 training script
code/isl_tutor/{__init__,data,features}.py   same normalisation code as the demo app
README.md                         contents + licence note
dataset-metadata.json             for the command-line upload only
```
The splits are FDMSE-ISL's own and are **signer-disjoint**:

| Split | Signers | Clips | Used for |
|---|---|---|---|
| train | 10 (s0001, s0002, s0005, s0007, s0008, s0009, s0011, s0012, s0013, s0018) | 20,016 | training |
| val | 2 (s0003, s0015) | 4,003 | choosing settings (search) + calibration |
| test | 8 (s0004, s0006, s0010, s0014, s0016, s0017, s0019, s0020) | 16,014 | **one** final evaluation |

## 2. Upload (private)

**Browser:** kaggle.com → **Datasets → + New Dataset**.
1. Drag in the **contents** of `kaggle\dataset\`, keeping `code/` as a folder.
2. Title: **`isl-sign-tutor-data`**.
3. Set visibility to **Private**. Never make it public, never share the link, never add collaborators.
4. Create, and wait for *"ready"*.

**Command line** (only with `kaggle.json` in `C:\Users\adity\.kaggle\`): replace `YOUR_KAGGLE_USERNAME` in `dataset-metadata.json`, then run
`kaggle datasets create -p E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\kaggle\dataset --dir-mode zip`. The metadata already sets `"isPrivate": true`.

## 3. Notebook settings

**Code → New Notebook**:

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Accelerator | **GPU T4 × 2** |
| Internet | **On** (pip installs) |
| Add Input | your private dataset `isl-sign-tutor-data` |

Run it with **Save Version → Save & Run All (Commit)**, so it keeps going with the browser closed and the outputs are saved.

Is Kaggle faster? For training, yes. For the first step, no:

  • Turning videos into keypoints stays on your laptop. Uploading the videos isn't allowed by the agreement, and Kaggle's 4 CPUs would be slower than yours anyway. Hands are found in 99.8% of frames.

Claude's response · 22 tool steps

Doing everything that improves accuracy or speed. Here's the full list and why none of it compromises the other:

  • More training data: left and right camera views of the training signers. This costs about 13 hours of extraction time on your laptop, but nothing in the final model's speed.
  • Self-supervised pretraining: re-download the unlabelled ISL pose data (NIOS, ISH News, Sign Library, about 30 GB) and pretrain on it.
  • Hand-shape features, measured relative to the wrist.
  • A graph-network member added to the ensemble.
  • Test-time flip averaging.
  • Longer training.
  • Speed: the recogniser runs once per sign (milliseconds), so even the ensemble with test-time averaging stays far below the tracking cost of about 58 ms per frame. I'll measure that rather than assume it.

/c/Users/adity/AppData/Local/Programs/Python/Python312/python.exe - <<'EOF'
p = "isl_tutor/data.py"; s = open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
old_feat = s[s.index("def features(pts, nm=None):"):s.index("def featurise(")]
new_feat = '''HAND_SLICES = (slice(25, 46), slice(46, 67))
LOCAL_DIM = 42 * 4                         # hand-local x, y + velocities for 2 x 21 hand points


def hand_local(pts):
    """(T,67,2) body-frame points -> (T,42,2) each hand relative to its wrist, scaled by palm size
    (wrist->middle-finger base). Makes handshape visible regardless of where the hand is. Absent hand -> 0."""
    out = []
    for sl in HAND_SLICES:
        h = pts[:, sl]
        size = np.linalg.norm(h[:, 9] - h[:, 0], axis=-1)[:, None, None]
        loc = np.clip((h - h[:, :1]) / (size + 1e-6), -5, 5)
        absent = (np.abs(h).sum((1, 2)) == 0) | (size[:, 0, 0] < 1e-4)
        loc[absent] = 0
        out.append(loc)
    return np.concatenate(out, 1).astype(np.float32)


def features(pts, nm=None, local=True):
    """pts (T,67,2) [+ nm (T,5)] -> (T, 67*4 [+ 42*4] [+10]) float32.
    Layout per point: [x, y, vx, vy]; then hand-local block (same layout); then non-manual + velocities."""
    vel = np.diff(pts, axis=0, prepend=pts[:1])
    out = [np.concatenate([pts, vel], -1).reshape(len(pts), -1)]
    if local:
        loc = hand_local(pts)
        lv = np.diff(loc, axis=0, prepend=loc[:1])
        out.append(np.concatenate([loc, lv], -1).reshape(len(pts), -1))
    if nm is not None:
        out.append(np.concatenate([nm, np.diff(nm, axis=0, prepend=nm[:1])], -1))
    return np.concatenate(out, -1).astype(np.float32)

input
"""ISL recogniser - best-results training on Kaggle GPU (T4 x2), FDMSE-ISL (2,002 signs, signer-disjoint splits).

Stages:  pretrain -> hpo -> final -> eval        (cells in KAGGLE.md)
  pretrain  masked-frame reconstruction on unlabelled ISL pose (NIOS, Sign Library, ISH News) -> transformer encoders
  hpo       Optuna TPE + median pruning over TWO model families (transformer 'tf', graph network 'gcn'),
            feature/augmentation/regularisation settings and optional side-view training data.
            Objective = top-1 on the 2 FDMSE val signers (never trained on).
  final     best tf config x seeds + best gcn config x seeds (EMA weights); ensemble weights, flip test-time
            augmentation and temperature chosen ON VAL; exported as one ONNX graph.
  eval      the one evaluation on the 8 FDMSE test signers + an ablation table (single model / ensemble / +gcn / +TTA)

Features (identical to the app, isl_tutor/data.py): per point [x,y,vx,vy] for 67 body+hand points in the signer's
body frame, + hand-local block (each hand relative to its wrist, scaled by palm size) [+ 5 non-manual + velocities].
Data hygiene: FDMSE test signers are touched ONLY by `eval`; side views come from TRAIN signers only.
"""
import argparse, copy, glob, json, math, os, pickle, random, re, sys, time
import numpy as np
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

DATA = os.environ.get("ISL_DATA", "/kaggle/input/isl-sign-tutor-data")
OUT = os.environ.get("ISL_OUT", "/kaggle/working/isl")
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(DATA, "code"))
from isl_tutor.data import FLIP, LOCAL_DIM, NM, P, features, resample  # noqa: E402  (same code as the app)

os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
LOG = os.path.join(OUT, "train.log")
D_BASE = P * 4                                       # 268
D_FEAT = D_BASE + LOCAL_DIM                          # 436 (without non-manual)


def d_in_of(use_nm):
    return D_FEAT + (NM * 2 if use_nm else 0)


def log(*a):
    s = " ".join(str(x) for x in a)
    print(s, flush=True)
    with open(LOG, "a") as fh:
        fh.write(time.strftime("%H:%M:%S ") + s + "\n")


def seed_all(s):
    random.seed(s); np.random.seed(s); torch.manual_seed(s); torch.cuda.manual_seed_all(s)


# ===================================================================================== data
def load_labelled():
    """labelled.pkl = {"classes": [...], "sets": {"train","val","test"[,"train_left","train_right"]: [clips]}}"""
    with open(os.path.join(DATA, "labelled.pkl"), "rb") as fh:
        d = pickle.load(fh)
    return d["classes"], d["sets"]


def train_items(sets, side):
    return sets["train"] + ((sets.get("train_left", []) + sets.get("train_right", [])) if side else [])

print("toy", {k: len(v) for k, v in sets.items()}, "classes", len(words), "bank", bank.shape)

    return keep, dropped

input
# Training the ISL recogniser on Kaggle (FDMSE-ISL, T4 × 2)

| Step | Where | Time |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Videos → keypoints: front view 40,033 clips + left/right views of the 10 train signers (~40,000 clips) | **your laptop** (videos must not leave it) | ~7 h + ~13 h, running |
| 2. Unlabelled ISL pose for pretraining (NIOS, ISH News, Sign Library) → cache | your laptop (I do this) | ~2 h |
| 3. Pack the keypoint dataset | your laptop (I do this) | minutes |
| 4. Upload as a **private** Kaggle dataset | you, in the browser | ~15 min |
| 5. Pretrain → search → ensemble → distil → evaluate | Kaggle T4 × 2 | an estimated 5–8 h (fits one 12-h session) |
| 6. Bring the model home, run the demo | your laptop | minutes |

> ⚠ **Licence gate — read before step 4.** The FDMSE-ISL agreement forbids redistribution, sharing and public hosting. The uploaded dataset contains **keypoints only** (no video, no frames), is **private**, and is used only by you. **Upload it only if the dataset authors agreed** in reply to your email. If not, tell me and we train on the laptop instead (no search; roughly 5–6 h per configuration).

## What the training does (and why each part is there)

| Technique | Why |
|---|---|
| Keypoints in the signer's body frame + **hand shape relative to the wrist** | handshape stays visible however big or far the signer is |
| **Self-supervised pretraining** on ~190 h of unlabelled ISL signing | learns how ISL moves before learning labels (+3.5 points on INCLUDE in OpenHands) |
| **Side camera views** of the train signers (search decides whether they help) | robustness to webcam angle |
| **Two model families**: 1D-CNN + transformer, and a spatio-temporal **graph network** | different errors → a better ensemble (the published SOTA on this dataset is a graph network) |
| Optuna search (40 trials), median pruning, AWP, EMA, late dropout, mirroring, speed/rotation/shear/masking augmentation | best settings, chosen only on the 2 **validation** signers |
| **Latency-aware ensemble** + optional flip test-time augmentation | members are added only while val accuracy rises **and** CPU time stays ≤ 60 ms per sign |
| **Distillation** into one fast student model | most of the ensemble's accuracy at single-model speed; the demo uses whichever is best on your laptop |
| One final **test on 8 unseen signers** + ablation table, against **HWGAT 93.86% top-1** (same split) | honest comparison with the state of the art |

---

## 1. The dataset (I build it when extraction finishes)

```
labelled.pkl          keypoints: train / val / test (official signer splits) + train_left / train_right
pretrain/oh_*.npy     unlabelled ISL pose banks (NIOS, ISH News, Sign Library)

code/isl_tutor/...    the app's own normalisation code
README.md, dataset-metadata.json
```
| Split | Signers | Clips | Used for |
|---|---|---|---|
| train (+ left/right views) | 10 | 20,016 (+ ~40,000 side-view) | training |
| val | 2 (s0003, s0015) | 4,003 | choosing settings, ensemble, calibration |
| test | 8 (s0004, s0006, s0010, s0014, s0016, s0017, s0019, s0020) | 16,014 | **one** final evaluation |

Command-line alternative (with `kaggle.json`): set your username in `dataset-metadata.json`, then run `kaggle datasets create -p E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\kaggle\dataset --dir-mode zip`.

Last bit of plumbing: a detached job that, once the pose download finishes, builds the pretraining cache and then deletes the roughly 41 GB of temporary HDF5 files it unpacks.

Write: E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\scripts\run_pretrain_cache.cmd
input
@echo off
REM Waits for the three OpenHands unlabelled-pose zips, builds the pretraining cache, then removes the
REM temporary HDF5 files (~41 GB). Log: data\processed\pretrain\build.log ; flag: data\processed\pretrain_cache.done
cd /d E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor
set D=data\raw\openhands_unlabelled_isl_poses
:wait
if not exist %D%\NIOS.zip ( timeout /t 120 /nobreak > nul & goto wait )
if not exist %D%\ISH_News.zip ( timeout /t 120 /nobreak > nul & goto wait )
if not exist %D%\Sign_Library.zip ( timeout /t 120 /nobreak > nul & goto wait )
if not exist data\processed\pretrain mkdir data\processed\pretrain
"E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\.venv\Scripts\python.exe" -u scripts\build_pretrain_cache.py openhands > data\processed\pretrain\build.log 2>&1
if exist data\interim rmdir /s /q data\interim
echo done > data\processed\pretrain_cache.done

Everything is set up. One last check is still running: the end-to-end test of the upgraded training pipeline.

Accuracy upgrades, all built and checked:

Upgrade Status
Hand-shape features: each hand relative to its wrist, scaled by palm size; the biggest gap versus Kaggle-winning solutions ✔ The training (GPU) features match the app's features exactly (difference 0.0)
Graph-network model added alongside the transformer; the published best on this dataset (HWGAT) is a graph network ✔ exports to ONNX; outputs match PyTorch (difference 3.6×10⁻⁷)
Side camera views of the 10 training signers as extra data; the search decides whether they help extraction queued after the front view; never from validation or test signers
Self-supervised pretraining on about 190 h of unlabelled ISL signing re-downloading now (6.5 of 29.9 GB); the cache builds automatically afterwards
Flip test-time averaging ✔ exactly equals recomputing on a mirrored skeleton (difference 0.0)
Longer training: 400 epochs for the final models; search over 40 trials and both model families ✔

Speed protections:

  • The graph network was too slow on your CPU at first: 54–171 ms per sign, against 9–25 ms for the transformers. I redesigned it to halve the frame rate inside the network, which should make it about 3–4× cheaper.
  • The final ensemble is latency-aware: members, and the flip averaging, are added only while validation accuracy improves and the total stays within 60 ms per sign.
  • Distillation: one fast model is also trained to imitate the whole ensemble. On your laptop I'll measure both models and use the best one that fits the speed budget.

import csv, os, glob, collections

npz = {os.path.basename(f)[:-4] for f in glob.glob("data/processed/fdmse_front/*.npz")}
meta = list(csv.DictReader(open("data/Raw/FDMSE-ISL/data_meta/metadata.csv", encoding="utf-8")))
want = {os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(r["video_dir"]))[0]: r["split"] for r in meta}
missing = [k for k in want if k not in npz]
print(f"metadata clips {len(want)} | npz files {len(npz)} | missing {len(missing)} {missing[:5]}")
print("failed rows:", len(bad), collections.Counter(r["status"][:40] for r in bad).most_common(3))
hr = [float(r["hand_rate"]) for r in ok.values()]
low = sum(h < 0.3 for h in hr)
print(f"hand found in frames: mean {sum(hr)/len(hr):.3f}; clips with hands <30% of frames: {low}")
print("per split:", collections.Counter(want[k] for k in npz if k in want))

Write: E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\scripts\eval_hwgat.py
input
"""Evaluate the published HWGAT checkpoint (sl-hwgat-demo, FDMSE-ISL, 2,002 signs) inside our pipeline.

HWGAT's own preprocessing code (third_party/sl-hwgat-demo/data_transform.py) is imported unchanged; only the
keypoint SOURCE differs: our MediaPipe Holistic-task keypoints at 12 fps instead of their legacy Holistic at 60 fps.

Conditions
  a  12fps      our keypoints as they are (the live-webcam setting)
  b  interp60   our keypoints linearly interpolated 12 -> 60 fps (closest to HWGAT's training input)
Splits: FDMSE test (8 signers) and val (2 signers). Writes models/hwgat_eval.json.
Usage: python scripts/eval_hwgat.py [--limit N] [--threads 4]
"""
import argparse, csv, json, os, sys, time
import numpy as np
import torch

ROOT = os.path.abspath(os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), ".."))
DEMO = os.path.join(ROOT, "third_party", "sl-hwgat-demo")
W = os.path.join(ROOT, "third_party", "hwgat_weights")
sys.path.insert(0, DEMO)
sys.path.insert(0, ROOT)
from data_transform import (Compose, HandCorrection, MediapipeDataProcess, NormalizeKeypoints,  # noqa: E402
                            PoseSelect, TemporalSample, WindowCreate)
from models.model_params import HWGATEParams  # noqa: E402
import models.HWGATE as HW  # noqa: E402
from isl_tutor.features import interp_nan  # noqa: E402

SRC_LEN = 192
KPS = [0, 2, 5, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15, 16] + [i + 33 + 468 for i in (0, 4, 5, 8, 9, 12, 13, 16, 17, 20)] + \
      [i + 21 + 33 + 468 for i in (0, 4, 5, 8, 9, 12, 13, 16, 17, 20)]
TRANSFORM = Compose([MediapipeDataProcess(), PoseSelect(KPS, [0, 1]), HandCorrection([9, 19, 7], [19, 29, 8]),
                     NormalizeKeypoints(0, [3, 4]), TemporalSample(SRC_LEN), WindowCreate(SRC_LEN)])
PROC = os.path.join(ROOT, "data", "processed", "fdmse_front")


def to_543(pose, lh, rh):
    """our arrays -> HWGAT's legacy Holistic layout (pose 0-32, face 33-500, left hand 501-521, right 522-542);
    a missing hand is all zeros, as in their extractor."""
    T = len(pose)
    f = np.zeros((T, 543, 4), np.float32)
    f[:, :33] = np.nan_to_num(pose)
    for h, s in ((lh, 501), (rh, 522)):
        ok = ~np.isnan(h[:, 0, 0])
        f[ok, s:s + 21, :3] = h[ok]
        f[ok, s:s + 21, 3] = 1.0
    return f


def upsample(pose, lh, rh, k=5):
    """12 fps -> 60 fps: fill detection gaps, then linear interpolation in time (hands only where seen)."""
    T = len(pose)
    t_new = np.linspace(0, T - 1, (T - 1) * k + 1)
    def lin(a):
        flat = a.reshape(T, -1)
        return np.stack([np.interp(t_new, np.arange(T), flat[:, j]) for j in range(flat.shape[1])], 1).reshape(len(t_new), *a.shape[1:])
    out = [lin(interp_nan(pose))]
    for h in (lh, rh):
        seen = ~np.isnan(h[:, 0, 0])
        if not seen.any():
            out.append(np.full((len(t_new),) + h.shape[1:], np.nan, np.float32)); continue
        hi = lin(interp_nan(h))

Write: E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\app\app_llm.py
input
"""Gloss sequence -> English: phrasebook first, then the local LLM (Ollama, qwen2.5:1.5b), then raw glosses.

Few-shot examples are given as separate chat turns (small models copy examples embedded in one prompt),
and an answer that merely repeats an example for a different input is rejected.
"""
import json, urllib.request

PHRASEBOOK = {"YOUR NAME WHAT": "What is your name?", "BANK WHERE": "Where is the bank?",
              "I FINE": "I am fine.", "THANK YOU": "Thank you.", "GOOD MORNING": "Good morning."}
SYSTEM = ("You translate Indian Sign Language (ISL) glosses into ONE short, natural English sentence. "
          "ISL order: time words first, verb last, question words and NOT at the end; ISL has no articles or tense. "
          "Use only the meaning of the given glosses. Reply with the English sentence only.")
SHOTS = [("TOMORROW I SCHOOL GO", "I will go to school tomorrow."),
         ("SHE APPLE RED LIKE NOT", "She does not like red apples."),
         ("YESTERDAY MY BROTHER MARKET GO", "My brother went to the market yesterday."),
         ("TRAIN WHEN", "When is the train?")]


def gloss_to_english(glosses, model="qwen2.5:1.5b"):
    key = " ".join(glosses).upper()
    if key in PHRASEBOOK:
        return PHRASEBOOK[key]
    msgs = [{"role": "system", "content": SYSTEM}]
    for g, e in SHOTS:
        msgs += [{"role": "user", "content": g}, {"role": "assistant", "content": e}]
    msgs.append({"role": "user", "content": key})
    try:
        req = urllib.request.Request("http://localhost:11434/api/chat", data=json.dumps(
            {"model": model, "messages": msgs, "stream": False,
             "options": {"temperature": 0, "num_predict": 40}}).encode(), headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
        out = json.load(urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=60))["message"]["content"].strip().split("\n")[0]
        if not out or any(out == e and key != g for g, e in SHOTS):      # copied an example -> don't trust it
            return key.capitalize()
        return out
    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - Ollama not running
        return key.capitalize()

"""Baseline: the published HWGAT checkpoint evaluated inside OUR pipeline (Kaggle GPU).

Uses HWGAT's own preprocessing code unchanged (dataset: code/hwgat/data_transform.py) on our MediaPipe
Holistic-task keypoints (raw_eval.pkl: image-space pose/hands at 12 fps for the val and test signers).
Conditions: '12fps' (live-webcam setting) and 'interp60' (12 -> 60 fps interpolation, closest to HWGAT's training).
Writes $ISL_OUT/hwgat_eval.json with the same metrics as our eval (top-1/5, per-signer, signer-bootstrap CI).
"""
import json, os, pickle, sys, time
from multiprocessing import Pool
import numpy as np
import torch

DATA = os.environ.get("ISL_DATA", "/kaggle/input/isl-sign-tutor-data")
OUT = os.environ.get("ISL_OUT", "/kaggle/working/isl")
HW = os.path.join(DATA, "code", "hwgat")
sys.path.insert(0, HW)
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(DATA, "code"))
SRC_LEN = 192
KPS = [0, 2, 5, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15, 16] + [i + 33 + 468 for i in (0, 4, 5, 8, 9, 12, 13, 16, 17, 20)] + \
      [i + 21 + 33 + 468 for i in (0, 4, 5, 8, 9, 12, 13, 16, 17, 20)]


def _transform():
    from data_transform import (Compose, HandCorrection, MediapipeDataProcess, NormalizeKeypoints, PoseSelect,
                                TemporalSample, WindowCreate)
    return Compose([MediapipeDataProcess(), PoseSelect(KPS, [0, 1]), HandCorrection([9, 19, 7], [19, 29, 8]),
                    NormalizeKeypoints(0, [3, 4]), TemporalSample(SRC_LEN), WindowCreate(SRC_LEN)])


def _interp_nan(x):
    T = len(x); flat = x.reshape(T, -1).copy(); ok = ~np.isnan(flat[:, 0])
    if ok.sum() == 0:
        return x
    for j in range(flat.shape[1]):
        flat[:, j] = np.interp(np.arange(T), np.arange(T)[ok], flat[ok, j])
    return flat.reshape(x.shape)


def _upsample(pose, lh, rh, k=5):
    T = len(pose); t_new = np.linspace(0, T - 1, (T - 1) * k + 1)
    def lin(a):
        flat = a.reshape(T, -1)
        return np.stack([np.interp(t_new, np.arange(T), flat[:, j]) for j in range(flat.shape[1])], 1).reshape(len(t_new), *a.shape[1:])
    out = [lin(_interp_nan(pose))]
    for h in (lh, rh):
        seen = ~np.isnan(h[:, 0, 0])
        if not seen.any():
            out.append(np.full((len(t_new),) + h.shape[1:], np.nan, np.float32)); continue
        hi = lin(_interp_nan(h)); hi[~(np.interp(t_new, np.arange(T), seen.astype(float)) > 0.5)] = np.nan
        out.append(hi)
    return [o.astype(np.float32) for o in out]


def _to_543(pose, lh, rh):
    f = np.zeros((len(pose), 543, 4), np.float32); f[:, :33] = np.nan_to_num(pose)
    for h, s in ((lh, 501), (rh, 522)):
        ok = ~np.isnan(h[:, 0, 0]); f[ok, s:s + 21, :3] = h[ok]; f[ok, s:s + 21, 3] = 1.0
    return f

It's slow on this CPU while the extraction runs (several seconds per clip), so the full 20,000-clip test runs on Kaggle's GPU instead. The quick local result on 200 clips will show early whether HWGAT holds up at webcam frame rates, and so whether to use it as the recogniser.

def read_manifest(folder, min_hand_rate):
    with open(os.path.join(folder, "manifest.csv"), encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        rows = [r for r in csv.DictReader(fh) if r["status"] == "ok"]
    seen, keep, dropped = set(), [], 0
    for r in rows:                                   # manifest is append-only: keep one row per id
        if r["id"] in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(r["id"])
        if float(r["hand_rate"]) < min_hand_rate:
            dropped += 1
            continue
        keep.append(r)
    return keep, dropped


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--min-hand-rate", type=float, default=0.3, help="drop clips where hands are almost never seen")
    a = ap.parse_args()

def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("source", choices=["islrtc", "include", "fdmse"])
    ap.add_argument("--view", default="front", help="fdmse: front|left|right")
    ap.add_argument("--splits", default="", help="fdmse: comma list, e.g. train (side views: train signers only)")
    ap.add_argument("--max-dur", type=float, default=0, help="islrtc: skip clips longer than this (s)")
    ap.add_argument("--workers", type=int, default=3)
    ap.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=0)
    ap.add_argument("--words-file", default=None, help="islrtc: JSON {word: [letter/file.mp4]} to extract")
    a = ap.parse_args()

    if a.limit:
        jobs = jobs[:a.limit]
    print(f"{len(jobs)} clips -> {out_dir}", flush=True)
    man = os.path.join(out_dir, "manifest.csv")
    fields = ["id", "word", "split", "frames", "fps", "active_start", "active_end", "hand_rate", "secs", "status"]
    new = not os.path.exists(man)
    t0, done = time.time(), 0
    with open(man, "a", newline="", encoding="utf-8") as fh, Pool(a.workers) as pool:
        w = csv.DictWriter(fh, fieldnames=fields, extrasaction="ignore")
        if new:
            w.writeheader()
        for r in pool.imap_unordered(worker, [(out_dir, j) for j in jobs], chunksize=2):
            if r is None:
                continue
            w.writerow(r); fh.flush(); done += 1
            if done % 50 == 0:

def worker(job):
    out_dir, job = job
    dst = os.path.join(out_dir, job["id"] + ".npz")
    if os.path.exists(dst):
        return None
    t0 = time.time()
    tmp = None
    try:
        path = job["src"]
        if job["zip"]:
            with zipfile.ZipFile(job["zip"]) as zf:
                fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(suffix=os.path.splitext(path)[1])
                with os.fdopen(fd, "wb") as fh:
                    fh.write(zf.read(path))
            path = tmp
        res = process(path)
        if res is None:
            return dict(job, status="decode_fail")
        np.savez_compressed(dst, **res)
        T = len(res["pose"])

# Training length is a fixed number of SAMPLES: "epochs" = passes over the front-view train set (N_FRONT clips),
# whatever the batch size and whether side views are added -> every trial costs the same and time is predictable.
N_FRONT = 20016
N_EVAL = 15                                          # validation checkpoints per run (pruning reports)
# Kaggle session end (unix time, set in cell 1): stages stop starting work that would not finish before it.
DEADLINE = float(os.environ.get("ISL_DEADLINE", "inf"))

+++ new
def load_labelled():
    """labelled.pkl = {"classes": [...], "sets": {"train","val","test"[,"train_left","train_right"]: [clips]}}"""
    global N_FRONT
    with open(os.path.join(DATA, "labelled.pkl"), "rb") as fh:
        d = pickle.load(fh)
    N_FRONT = len(d["sets"]["train"])
    return d["classes"], d["sets"]


def sync(dev):
    if str(dev).startswith("cuda"):
        torch.cuda.synchronize(dev)


def hours_left():
    return (DEADLINE - time.time()) / 3600

+++ new
def pretrain(a, probe_steps=0):
    """probe_steps > 0: time that many steps per size, save nothing, return {size: sec/step} (speed check)."""
    dev, T = a.device, 48
    files = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(DATA, "pretrain", "*_index.npy")))
    if not files:
        log("no pretrain/ folder in the dataset -> skipping pretraining (search uses pretrained=False)")
        return {}
    banks, idxs, off = [], [], 0

def train_steps(cfg, epochs):
    """budget in front-view passes -> optimizer steps (same number of samples for any batch size / side setting)."""
    return max(1, math.ceil(epochs * N_FRONT / cfg.get("bs", 64)))


def train_one(cfg, items, val_sets, classes, dev, seed=0, trial=None, epochs=None, teacher=None, kd_alpha=0.7, kd_t=2.0,
              steps=None):
    seed_all(seed)
    cid = {w: i for i, w in enumerate(classes)}
    T, use_nm = cfg["T"], cfg["use_nm"]
    model = make_model(cfg, len(classes)).to(dev)
    ema = copy.deepcopy(model).eval()
    for p in ema.parameters():
        p.requires_grad_(False)
    ds = GPUSet(items, cid, dev)
    vX = {k: (eval_features(v, T, use_nm).to(dev), torch.tensor([cid[x["word"]] for x in v], device=dev))
          for k, v in val_sets.items() if v}
    bs = min(cfg.get("bs", 64), ds.n)
    total = steps or train_steps(cfg, epochs or cfg["epochs"])
    checks = {max(1, round(total * (r + 1) / N_EVAL)): r for r in range(N_EVAL)}     # step -> report index
    heads = list(model.head.parameters())
    base = [p for n, p in model.named_parameters() if not n.startswith("head")]
    lr_enc = cfg["lr"] * (cfg["enc_lr_mult"] if cfg.get("pretrained") else 1)
    opt = torch.optim.AdamW([{"params": base, "lr": lr_enc}, {"params": heads, "lr": cfg["lr"]}], weight_decay=cfg["wd"])
    sched = torch.optim.lr_scheduler.OneCycleLR(opt, [lr_enc, cfg["lr"]], total_steps=total,
                                                pct_start=0.1 if total >= 50 else 0.3)
    res, pos, perm, t_probe = {}, ds.n, None, None
    model.train()
    for step in range(total):
        if step == min(10, total - 1):

def final(a):
    import optuna
    classes, sets = load_labelled()
    cid = {w: i for i, w in enumerate(classes)}
    study = optuna.load_study(study_name="isl", storage=f"sqlite:///{OUT}/optuna.db")

    cfg_gc, t_gc = best_cfg_of(study, "gcn")
    lead = cfg_tf if (t_gc is None or (t_tf and t_tf.value >= t_gc.value)) else cfg_gc
    T, use_nm = lead["T"], lead["use_nm"]                       # one input format for the whole ensemble
    specs = []
    for cfg, n in ((cfg_tf, a.seeds), (cfg_gc, a.gcn_seeds)):
        if cfg is None or n == 0:
            continue

def final_specs(a):
    """The ensemble's member list, derived only from the search results + args (identical in every process)."""
    import optuna
    study = optuna.load_study(study_name="isl", storage=f"sqlite:///{OUT}/optuna.db")
    cfg_tf, t_tf = best_cfg_of(study, "tf")
    cfg_gc, t_gc = best_cfg_of(study, "gcn")
    lead = cfg_tf if (t_gc is None or (t_tf and t_tf.value >= t_gc.value)) else cfg_gc
    T, use_nm = lead["T"], lead["use_nm"]                       # one input format for the whole ensemble
    specs = []
    for cfg, n in ((cfg_tf, a.seeds), (cfg_gc, a.gcn_seeds)):
        if cfg is None or n == 0:
            continue
        cfg = dict(cfg, T=T, use_nm=use_nm, epochs=a.final_epochs)
        specs += [dict(cfg, seed=s) for s in range(n)]
    return specs, T, use_nm, t_tf, t_gc


def train_members(a, specs, sets, classes, shard=(0, 1)):
    """Train this shard's members (i % n == k); members already saved are skipped, so a cut-off session resumes."""
    k, n = shard
    longest = 0.0
    for i, cfg in enumerate(specs):
        path = os.path.join(OUT, f"member_{i}.pt")
        if i % n != k or os.path.exists(path):
            continue
        if time.time() + 1.15 * longest > DEADLINE:
            log(f"[{a.device}] member {i} not started: it would not finish before the session ends ({hours_left():.2f} h left)")
            continue
        t0 = time.time()
        items = train_items(sets, cfg["side"]) + (sets["val"] if a.final_with_val else [])

# ===================================================================================== speed check
def bench(a):
    """Cell 1b: time real training steps on this GPU and print how long every cell will take with the current args,
    and how many search trials fit in session 1. Saves nothing except bench.json."""
    classes, sets = load_labelled()
    items = sets["train"][:4000]                               # cost per step does not depend on the dataset size
    probe = dict(lr=1e-3, wd=0.05, ls=0.1, rot=15, scale=0.15, shear=0.1, speed=0.3, mask_p=0.4, awp=0.01, late_drop=0.3,
                 drop=0.2, head_drop=0.4, ema=0.999, enc_lr_mult=1.0, pretrained=False, use_nm=True, side=False)
    shapes = {"tf small": dict(arch="tf", d=128, depth=2, T=32, bs=64), "tf mid": dict(arch="tf", d=256, depth=3, T=48, bs=64),
              "tf large": dict(arch="tf", d=320, depth=4, T=64, bs=128),
              "gcn small": dict(arch="gcn", d=128, depth=2, T=32, bs=64), "gcn mid": dict(arch="gcn", d=256, depth=3, T=48, bs=64),
              "gcn large": dict(arch="gcn", d=320, depth=4, T=64, bs=128)}
    per_sample = {}
    for name, s in shapes.items():
        cfg = dict(probe, **s)
        _, res = train_one(cfg, items, {}, classes, a.device, steps=a.bench_steps)
        per_sample[name] = res["sec_per_step"] / cfg["bs"]
        log(f"speed {name:9s}: {res['sec_per_step']*1000:.0f} ms/step (bs {cfg['bs']}) -> "
            f"{per_sample[name] * N_FRONT * a.epochs / 60:.1f} min per {a.epochs}-epoch search trial")
    torch.cuda.empty_cache() if str(a.device).startswith("cuda") else None
    pt = pretrain(a, probe_steps=a.bench_steps)
    for k, v in pt.items():
        log(f"speed pretrain {k}: {v*1000:.0f} ms/step -> {v * a.pt_steps / 60:.1f} min for {a.pt_steps} steps")
    h = 1 / 3600
    pre_h = sum(pt.values()) * a.pt_steps * h / 2                               # cell 2: sizes split over 2 GPUs
    trial_full = np.mean(list(per_sample.values())) * N_FRONT * a.epochs * h    # average search trial, not pruned
    trial_exp = trial_full * 0.7                                                # median pruning stops weak trials early
    tf_m = np.mean([v for k, v in per_sample.items() if k.startswith("tf")])
    gc_m = np.mean([v for k, v in per_sample.items() if k.startswith("gcn")])
    member = {"tf": tf_m * N_FRONT * a.final_epochs * h, "gcn": gc_m * N_FRONT * a.final_epochs * h}

import sys,pickle,numpy as np
S=sys.argv[1]; rng=np.random.default_rng(0)
cls=[f"w{i}" for i in range(6)]
def clip(w,sg):
    L=rng.integers(20,50); return dict(pts=rng.normal(0,1,(L,67,2)).astype(np.float32),nm=rng.normal(0,1,(L,5)).astype(np.float32),word=w,signer=sg,id="x")

def hours_left():

def bench_sec(key, arch=None):
    """Worst-case seconds from the speed check (bench.json): key 'trial' = longest search trial, 'member' = one final
    member of `arch`. 0 when there is no speed check."""
    p = os.path.join(OUT, "bench.json")
    if not os.path.exists(p):
        return 0.0
    b = json.load(open(p))
    if key == "trial":
        return max(b["sec_per_sample"].values()) * b.get("n_front", N_FRONT) * b["epochs"]
    return b["member_h"][arch] * 3600 * b["final_epochs_ratio"]

+++ new
def bench_sec(epochs, arch=None):
    """Worst-case seconds for one training run of `epochs` (largest model of `arch`, or of both families), from the
    speed check (bench.json). 0 when there was no speed check."""
    p = os.path.join(OUT, "bench.json")
    if not os.path.exists(p):
        return 0.0
    ps = json.load(open(p))["sec_per_sample"]
    return max(v for k, v in ps.items() if arch is None or k.startswith(arch + " ")) * N_FRONT * epochs

14	## What the training does (and why each part is there)
15	
16	| Technique | Why |
17	|---|---|
18	| Keypoints in the signer's body frame + **hand shape relative to the wrist** | handshape stays visible however big or far the signer is |
19	| **Self-supervised pretraining** on ~190 h of unlabelled ISL signing | learns how ISL moves before learning labels (+3.5 points on INCLUDE in OpenHands) |
20	| **Side camera views** of the train signers (search decides whether they help) | robustness to webcam angle |
21	| **Two model families**: 1D-CNN + transformer, and a spatio-temporal **graph network** | different errors → a better ensemble (the published SOTA on this dataset is a graph network) |
22	| Optuna search (40 trials), median pruning, AWP, EMA, late dropout, mirroring, speed/rotation/shear/masking augmentation | best settings, chosen only on the 2 **validation** signers |
23	| **Latency-aware ensemble** + optional flip test-time augmentation | members are added only while val accuracy rises **and** CPU time stays ≤ 60 ms per sign |
24	| **Distillation** into one fast student model | most of the ensemble's accuracy at single-model speed; the demo uses whichever is best on your laptop |
25	| One final **test on 8 unseen signers** + ablation table, against **HWGAT 93.86% top-1** (same split) | honest comparison with the state of the art |

## 3. Two sessions
The whole run takes longer than one Kaggle session (12 h), so it runs as **two notebooks**, one after the other. Each one is committed with **Save Version → Save & Run All (Commit)**, so it keeps running with the browser closed and its outputs are kept.

| | Notebook | Cells | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Session 1 | `isl-train-1` | 1, 1b, 2, 3 | speed check, pretraining, search |
| Session 2 | `isl-train-2` | 1, 1c, 4a, 4, 5, 6, 6b, 7 | final models, distillation, evaluation |

Settings for **both** notebooks (**Code → New Notebook**):

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Accelerator | **GPU T4 × 2** |
| Internet | **On** |
| Add Input | `isl-sign-tutor-data` (session 2 also adds `isl-train-1`; see cell 1c) |

**Session-end guard.** Cell 1 records when the session started. The search and the final training never start a model that, by the speed check's worst-case estimate, would not finish before the 12-hour limit. Everything finished so far is saved, and a later session picks up from there.

```python
import os, subprocess, sys, pickle, glob, time, json
found = glob.glob("/kaggle/input/**/labelled.pkl", recursive=True)
assert found, "dataset not attached: Add Input -> isl-sign-tutor-data"
os.environ["ISL_DATA"] = os.path.dirname(found[0])
os.environ["ISL_OUT"]  = "/kaggle/working/isl"
os.environ["ISL_DEADLINE"] = str(time.time() + 11.3 * 3600)    # stop starting work ~40 min before the 12 h limit
os.makedirs(os.environ["ISL_OUT"], exist_ok=True)
subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "pip", "install", "-q", "optuna", "onnx", "onnxruntime"], check=True)
print(subprocess.run(["nvidia-smi", "--query-gpu=name,memory.total", "--format=csv"], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout)
D = os.environ["ISL_DATA"]; OUT = os.environ["ISL_OUT"]; TRAIN = f"{D}/code/isl_train.py"
lab = pickle.load(open(f"{D}/labelled.pkl", "rb"))
print(len(lab["classes"]), "classes |", {k: len(v) for k, v in lab["sets"].items()})
print({k: sorted({c["signer"] for c in v}) for k, v in lab["sets"].items() if k in ("train", "val", "test")})
print(sorted(os.listdir(f"{D}/pretrain")))
del lab
env0 = dict(os.environ, CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="0"); env1 = dict(os.environ, CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="1")
def both(args0, args1):                       # one process per GPU, wait for both
    p0 = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, TRAIN, *args0], env=env0); p1 = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, TRAIN, *args1], env=env1)
    print("exit codes", p0.wait(), p1.wait())
```

Shell cwd was reset to e:\AIDS\Project

All four changes are done and tested, and the dataset is ready to upload: isl-sign-tutor\kaggle\dataset\, 4.13 GB. I ran every stage on the laptop with a small fake dataset; they all worked, including the two-GPU split, resuming after a cut-off and the stop before the session limit. The scores from that run mean nothing because the data was random; only the plumbing was being tested.

What changed:

  1. Final models on both GPUs: the seven final models are split across the two GPUs (four on one, three on the other), which roughly halves that step. Each model is saved as soon as it finishes, so if a session ends early, the next one trains only the missing ones.
  2. Fixed training length: every run now sees the same number of samples, whatever its batch size or whether side views are added. Before, a side-view trial took about 3× longer than a front-only one; now trials are compared fairly and their time is predictable.
  3. Speed check (cell 1b, ~10 min): it times the real models on Kaggle's T4 and prints how long each cell will take and how many search trials fit in session 1. If it shows FEW TRIALS or TOO LONG, stop the run and paste me that block.
  4. Two sessions: session 1 does pretraining and the search. Session 2 takes session 1's results as an input and does the final models, the student model and the evaluation. A built-in guard never starts a model that wouldn't finish before Kaggle's 12-hour limit, so a session is never killed with its results unsaved.

KAGGLE.md now has the full steps. It also finds the dataset path by itself, so you don't need to edit any paths.

Your next steps:

  1. Upload the contents of kaggle\dataset\ as a Private dataset named isl-sign-tutor-data (KAGGLE.md section 2).
  2. Create notebook isl-train-1 with GPU T4 × 2 and Internet on, paste cells 1, 1b, 2 and 3, then use Save & Run All (Commit).
  3. When it finishes, create isl-train-2, add isl-train-1's output as an input, and run cells 1, 1c, 4a, 4, 5, 6, 6b and 7.

1	# Training the ISL recogniser on Kaggle (FDMSE-ISL, T4 × 2)
2	
3	| Step | Where | Time |
4	|---|---|---|
5	| 1. Videos → keypoints: front view 40,033 clips + left/right views of the 10 train signers (~40,000 clips) | **your laptop** (videos must not leave it) | done |
6	| 2. Unlabelled ISL pose for pretraining (NIOS, ISH News, Sign Library) → cache | your laptop (I do this) | done |
7	| 3. Pack the keypoint dataset (4.1 GB) | your laptop (I do this) | done |
8	| 4. Upload as a **private** Kaggle dataset | you, in the browser | ~15 min |
9	| 5. Session 1: speed check → pretrain → search. Session 2: ensemble → distil → evaluate | Kaggle T4 × 2 | two sessions of up to 12 h (see *Time*) |
10	| 6. Bring the model home, run the demo | your laptop | minutes |
11	
12	> ⚠ **Licence gate — read before step 4.** The FDMSE-ISL agreement forbids redistribution, sharing and public hosting. The uploaded dataset contains **keypoints only** (no video, no frames), is **private**, and is used only by you. **Upload it only if the dataset authors agreed** in reply to your email. If not, tell me and we train on the laptop instead (no search; roughly 5–6 h per configuration).
13	
14	## What the training does (and why each part is there)
15	
16	| Technique | Why |
17	|---|---|
18	| Keypoints in the signer's body frame + **hand shape relative to the wrist** | handshape stays visible however big or far the signer is |
19	| **Self-supervised pretraining** on ~190 h of unlabelled ISL signing | learns how ISL moves before learning labels (+3.5 points on INCLUDE in OpenHands) |
20	| **Side camera views** of the train signers (search decides whether they help) | robustness to webcam angle |
21	| **Two model families**: 1D-CNN + transformer, and a spatio-temporal **graph network** | different errors → a better ensemble (the published SOTA on this dataset is a graph network) |
22	| Optuna search (up to 40 trials, equal training budget each), median pruning, AWP, EMA, late dropout, mirroring, speed/rotation/shear/masking augmentation | best settings, chosen only on the 2 **validation** signers |
23	| **Latency-aware ensemble** + optional flip test-time augmentation | members are added only while val accuracy rises **and** CPU time stays ≤ 60 ms per sign |
24	| **Distillation** into one fast student model | most of the ensemble's accuracy at single-model speed; the demo uses whichever is best on your laptop |
25	| One final **test on 8 unseen signers** + ablation table, against **HWGAT 93.86% top-1** (same split) | honest comparison with the state of the art |

input
S="C:/Users/adity/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/e--AIDS-Project/4126878e-dddd-4cf9-baea-f10a041f0800/scratchpad/smoke"

.venv/Scripts/python.exe - "$S" <<'EOF'
import sys, pickle, numpy as np
S = sys.argv[1]; rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
cls = [f"w{i}" for i in range(6)]
def clip(w, sg):
    L = rng.integers(20, 50)
    return dict(pts=rng.normal(0, 1, (L, 67, 2)).astype(np.float32), nm=rng.normal(0, 1, (L, 5)).astype(np.float32), word=w, signer=sg, id="x")
sets = {"train": [clip(w, f"s{k}") for w in cls for k in range(8)], "val": [clip(w, "sv") for w in cls for k in range(3)],
        "test": [clip(w, f"st{k}") for w in cls for k in range(4)]}
sets["train_left"] = sets["train"][:20]; sets["train_right"] = sets["train"][:20]
pickle.dump({"classes": cls, "sets": sets}, open(S + "/data/labelled.pkl", "wb"))
for n in ("a", "b"):
    np.save(f"{S}/data/pretrain/oh_{n}.npy", rng.normal(0, 1, (3000, 67, 2)).astype(np.float16))
    np.save(f"{S}/data/pretrain/oh_{n}_index.npy", np.array([[i * 100, 100] for i in range(30)]))
print("fake dataset built")
EOF
