Yes, you can start now. Before saying so I ran a final check and fixed two problems that could have crashed the run partway through. Everything was re-tested on a small fake dataset, and the updated code is in kaggle\dataset\.

What I fixed:

  • NumPy version: the data files were saved with NumPy 2.4. If Kaggle's image had NumPy 1.x, every step would have failed at the first data load. The code now reads them on either version. That fallback couldn't be tested here because the laptop only has NumPy 2, but cell 1 prints Kaggle's NumPy version.
  • GPU memory: the training data with side views takes 3.2 GB on the GPU, and the largest model setting could reach about 12 of the T4's 16 GB. A search trial that runs out of memory is now skipped instead of stopping the whole search; I tested this with a simulated failure.
  • Earlier warning: the speed check now runs the largest models on the full side-view data and prints peak GPU memory. A memory problem will show up in the first 10 minutes, not hours in.
  • Upload check: cell 1 now stops with a clear message if the upload flattened the code/ and pretrain/ folders.

What to do (all in KAGGLE.md):

  1. Upload: on kaggle.com go to Datasets → New Dataset. Upload the contents of E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\kaggle\dataset\ (3.9 GB) and keep the code and pretrain folders. Name it isl-sign-tutor-data and set it to Private.
  2. Session 1: create a new notebook called isl-train-1, set GPU T4 × 2 and Internet On, and add the dataset as input. Paste cells 1, 1b, 2 and 3, then use Save Version → Save & Run All (Commit). It runs for up to about 12 h and you can close the browser.
  3. Check after about 20 minutes: open the running version's log and paste me the cell 1 and cell 1b output (the speed ... lines and the ESTIMATE/SESSION block). That confirms the timing and memory on the real GPU before most of the hours are spent.
  4. Session 2: when session 1 has finished, create isl-train-2 with isl-train-1's output as an extra input. Run cells 1, 1c, 4a, 4, 5, 6, 6b and 7, then download isl_outputs.zip.

"""Local check of the Kaggle-trained recognisers (run from the project root):
accuracy on FDMSE val/test with the APP's feature code, temperature refit on val, laptop CPU latency."""
import json, os, pickle, sys, time
import numpy as np
import onnxruntime as ort

ROOT = os.getcwd()
sys.path.insert(0, ROOT)
from isl_tutor.data import features, resample  # noqa: E402  (the same code the app uses)

M = os.path.join(ROOT, "models", "best", "isl_outputs")
with open(os.path.join(ROOT, "kaggle", "dataset", "labelled.pkl"), "rb") as fh:
    d = pickle.load(fh)
classes, sets = d["classes"], {k: d["sets"][k] for k in ("val", "test")}
del d
cid = {w: i for i, w in enumerate(classes)}
out = {}


def feats(items, T, use_nm):
    return np.stack([features(resample(it["pts"], T), resample(it["nm"], T) if use_nm else None)
                     for it in items]).astype(np.float32)


def nll(lo, y, t):
    z = lo / t
    z = z - z.max(1, keepdims=True)
    return float(np.mean(np.log(np.exp(z).sum(1)) - z[np.arange(len(y)), y]))


def ece(lo, y, t, bins=15):
    z = lo / t; z = z - z.max(1, keepdims=True); p = np.exp(z); p /= p.sum(1, keepdims=True)
    conf, pred = p.max(1), p.argmax(1)
    e = 0.0
    for a, b in zip(np.linspace(0, 1, bins + 1)[:-1], np.linspace(0, 1, bins + 1)[1:]):
        m = (conf > a) & (conf <= b)
        if m.any():
            e += m.mean() * abs((pred[m] == y[m]).mean() - conf[m].mean())
    return float(e)


so = ort.SessionOptions(); so.intra_op_num_threads = 4
for name in ("recognizer", "recognizer_student"):
    meta = json.load(open(os.path.join(M, f"{name}_meta.json")))
    T, use_nm = meta["T"], meta["use_nm"]
    assert meta["classes"] == classes, "class order differs from labelled.pkl"
    sess = ort.InferenceSession(os.path.join(M, f"{name}.onnx"), so, providers=["CPUExecutionProvider"])
    res = {}
    for split in ("val", "test"):
        X = feats(sets[split], T, use_nm)
        y = np.array([cid[it["word"]] for it in sets[split]])
        lo = np.concatenate([sess.run(None, {"x": X[i:i + 256]})[0] for i in range(0, len(X), 256)])
        top5 = np.argsort(-lo, 1)[:, :5]
        res[split] = dict(n=len(y), top1=round(float((top5[:, 0] == y).mean()), 4),
                          top5=round(float((top5 == y[:, None]).any(1).mean()), 4))
        if split == "val":
            temps = np.exp(np.linspace(np.log(0.02), np.log(4.0), 120))
            t_best = float(temps[np.argmin([nll(lo, y, t) for t in temps])])
            res["temperature"] = dict(kaggle=meta["temperature"], refit=round(t_best, 4),
                                      val_nll_kaggle=round(nll(lo, y, meta["temperature"]), 4),
… 20 more lines not shown
output

1. Can you delete the dataset from Kaggle? Yes. Everything is verified locally, and the correction feature doesn't need any more GPU training.

  • Delete isl-sign-tutor-data (the dataset page → ⋮ / Settings → Delete). That's the FDMSE-derived data, and removing it honours the licence.
  • The isl-train-1 and isl-train-2 notebooks contain no raw data, but their outputs include models trained on FDMSE. Everything in them is already on your laptop (models\best\ and models\session 1\). Deleting them is the cleanest option for the licence, but not required; they're private.
  • isl-sign-tutor-code is only our script. You can keep or delete it.
  • If we ever need Kaggle training again, the zip is still on your laptop (kaggle\isl-sign-tutor-data.zip), so it would mean one re-upload.

2. Next: the teaching and correction part. None of it needs Kaggle; it's all built and tested on your laptop.

Step What How
1. Reference library how native signers make each of the 2,002 words From the 10 training signers' keypoints (front and side views), compute each word's typical version and how much native signers naturally vary. No training, only computation, and it stays private on your laptop.
2. The four parameters handshape, palm orientation, location, movement Measure each from the keypoints: finger angles, palm direction, hand position relative to body zones (chin, chest, etc.), and the movement path. Most of this code exists in isl_tutor/features.py.
3. The checker your attempt vs the reference Time-align your sign with the reference, then compare each parameter with the native signers' own spread. Feedback is specific, e.g. "handshape ✓, palm should face outward, hand too low (chest instead of chin), movement too short".
4. Measuring it honestly does the feedback catch real errors without nagging correct signs? (a) False alarms: run the checker on correct signs from the 8 test signers it never saw; it should rarely complain. (b) Error detection: create controlled mistakes in correct signs (turn the palm, move the hand to a different body zone, swap the handshape, shorten the movement) and measure how often the checker names the right parameter.
5. Practice screen the demo Pick a word → watch the skeleton reference → sign it → see the recognised word, plus per-parameter feedback drawn on your own skeleton.
6. Later real learners Check the feedback with an ISL teacher or learners (task 4.x).

Step 4 gives you numbers for your professor's question: a tutor that sees your hand at webcam speed, offline, and tells you which of the four parameters is off, with measured accuracy. That's something ChatGPT can't do.

Shall I start with steps 1–4 now? They run entirely on the laptop, and the evaluation in step 4 tells us whether the correction is good enough before I build the screen.

Turn 16

continue with the next part as you have everything needed for the correcting part of the model.

"""Feature B: compare a learner's sign with a reference sign, parameter by parameter.

compare(learner_npz, reference_npz) -> dict with an overall DTW cost, a per-parameter verdict
(handshape, orientation, location, movement) and plain-language corrections.

with calibrate_thresholds().

import numpy as np

                       palm_normal, FINGERS)

THRESH = {"handshape_deg": 25.0, "orientation_deg": 40.0, "location_sw": 0.25, "movement_cost": 0.35}


def dtw_path(a, b):
    """Classic DTW on frame-feature sequences a (n,d), b (m,d). Returns (normalised cost, path)."""
    n, m = len(a), len(b)
    d = np.linalg.norm(a[:, None, :] - b[None, :, :], axis=-1)
    D = np.full((n + 1, m + 1), np.inf)
    D[0, 0] = 0
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        for j in range(1, m + 1):
            D[i, j] = d[i - 1, j - 1] + min(D[i - 1, j], D[i, j - 1], D[i - 1, j - 1])
    i, j, path = n, m, []
    while i > 0 and j > 0:
        path.append((i - 1, j - 1))
        k = np.argmin([D[i - 1, j - 1], D[i - 1, j], D[i, j - 1]])
        i, j = (i - 1, j - 1) if k == 0 else (i - 1, j) if k == 1 else (i, j - 1)
    return D[n, m] / (n + m), path[::-1]

def active_segment(pose, lh, rh, fps):
    """Signing = a hand detected above the resting position. Returns padded (start, end) indices."""
    hip_y = np.nanmean(pose[:, [23, 24], 1], axis=1)
    sh_y = np.nanmean(pose[:, [11, 12], 1], axis=1)
    rest_y = hip_y - 0.25 * (hip_y - sh_y)               # wrists above this line count as raised
    raised = (pose[:, 15, 1] < rest_y) | (pose[:, 16, 1] < rest_y)
    hand = ~np.isnan(lh[:, 0, 0]) | ~np.isnan(rh[:, 0, 0])
    act = raised & hand
    if not act.any():
        return 0, len(pose) - 1
    idx = np.flatnonzero(act)
    pad = int(round(0.25 * fps))
    return max(0, idx[0] - pad), min(len(pose) - 1, idx[-1] + pad)

"""Sign correction: compare a learner's attempt with the native signers of the same word, parameter by parameter
(handshape, palm/finger orientation, location, movement, one or two hands), and say what to fix.

Reference library (scripts/build_references.py): every word's renditions by the 10 FDMSE train signers, reduced to
per-frame parameter streams, plus how much those native signers differ from EACH OTHER (leave-one-out spread).
A learner's parameter is flagged only when it is clearly further from the natives than the natives are from one
another (ratio > a threshold tuned on the 2 FDMSE val signers; measured on the 8 test signers by
scripts/eval_correction.py).

Coordinates: MediaPipe image-normalised landmarks; x (and hand z, which MediaPipe scales like x) are multiplied by
the frame's aspect ratio so angles are true. Everything is mirrored so that the dominant hand is a right hand
(left-handed learners are compared fairly). Body frame: origin = shoulder midpoint, unit = shoulder width, y down.
"""
import json, os

import numpy as np

from .features import FINGERS, interp_nan, joint_angles, location_zone

N = 24                                   # frames per sign after time normalisation
BAND = 6                                 # DTW may shift timing by up to 6 of 24 frames
FDMSE_ASPECT = 1200 / 950                # FDMSE videos are 1200 x 950
K_NEAR = 3                               # each parameter is judged against the 3 closest native renditions
SUBS = ("handshape", "palm", "fingers", "location", "move_size", "move_shape")
PARAM_OF = {"handshape": "handshape", "palm": "orientation", "fingers": "orientation", "location": "location",
            "move_size": "movement", "move_shape": "movement"}
PARAMS = ("handshape", "orientation", "location", "movement", "hands")
ROOT = os.path.abspath(os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), ".."))
LIBRARY = os.path.join(ROOT, "models", "best", "reference_library.npz")


def _unit(v):
    return v / (np.linalg.norm(v, axis=-1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9)


def _resample(x, n=N):
    T = len(x)
    if T == 1:
        return np.repeat(x, n, 0)
    t = np.linspace(0, T - 1, n)
    i0 = np.floor(t).astype(int); i1 = np.minimum(i0 + 1, T - 1)
    w = (t - i0).reshape((n,) + (1,) * (x.ndim - 1))
    return x[i0] * (1 - w) + x[i1] * w


# ===================================================================================== attempt -> parameter streams

    Returns dict(P (T,33,2), D (T,21,3) dominant hand, ND (T,21,3) other hand, mirrored)."""

    def hand(a):
        a = np.array(a[..., :3], np.float32); a[..., 0] *= aspect; a[..., 2] *= aspect
        return a
    L, R = hand(lh), hand(rh)
    rest = _rest_line_y(P)

    def activity(h):                         # frames raised above the rest line, then how far the wrist travels
        w = h[:, 0, :2]; ok = ~np.isnan(w[:, 0])
        raised = ok & (w[:, 1] < rest)

"""Build the correction reference library from the FDMSE TRAIN signers (front view) -> models/best/reference_library.npz

Per word: the native renditions as parameter streams (isl_tutor.correct.streams), whether each uses two hands,
the leave-one-out spread (how far each native is from the others, per sub-parameter), the medoid (most typical
rendition) and its body-frame skeleton for the "Show me" animation. Private: derived from FDMSE-ISL.
Usage: python scripts/build_references.py [--workers 6]
"""
import argparse, csv, json, os, sys, time
from multiprocessing import Pool

import numpy as np

ROOT = os.path.abspath(os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), ".."))
sys.path.insert(0, ROOT)
from isl_tutor.correct import LIBRARY, N, SUBS, _nearest_mean, attempt_streams, pair_distances  # noqa: E402
from isl_tutor.data import points_from_arrays  # noqa: E402

PROC = os.path.join(ROOT, "data", "processed", "fdmse_front")
TRAIN = {"s0001", "s0002", "s0005", "s0007", "s0008", "s0009", "s0011", "s0012", "s0013", "s0018"}


def manifest(split_signers=None):
    """{word: [clip id, ...]} from the extraction manifest (one row per id), optionally only some signers."""
    seen, out = set(), {}
    with open(os.path.join(PROC, "manifest.csv"), encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        for r in csv.DictReader(fh):
            if r["status"] != "ok" or r["id"] in seen:
                continue
            seen.add(r["id"])
            if split_signers is None or r["id"][:5] in split_signers:
                out.setdefault(r["word"], []).append(r["id"])
    return {w: sorted(v) for w, v in out.items()}


def load_clip(cid):
    d = np.load(os.path.join(PROC, cid + ".npz"))
    s, e = d["active"]
    return tuple(d[k][s:e + 1].astype(np.float32) for k in ("pose", "lh", "rh"))


def _job(cid):
    pose, lh, rh = load_clip(cid)
    return cid, attempt_streams(pose, lh, rh)


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--workers", type=int, default=6)
    a = ap.parse_args()
    t0 = time.time()
    by_word = manifest(TRAIN)
    words = sorted(by_word)
    ids = [c for w in words for c in by_word[w]]
    with Pool(a.workers) as pool:
        st = dict(pool.imap_unordered(_job, ids, chunksize=64))
    print(f"{len(ids)} native clips -> streams in {time.time() - t0:.0f} s; unusable (hand not seen): "
          f"{sum(v is None for v in st.values())}", flush=True)
    W, K = len(words), max(len(v) for v in by_word.values())
    arr = {k: np.full((W, K, N) + shp, np.nan, np.float16) for k, shp in
           (("ang", (15,)), ("normal", (3,)), ("fdir", (3,)), ("loc", (2,)), ("traj", (2,)))}

"""Tune and measure the sign corrector (isl_tutor/correct.py) without learner recordings.

1. Thresholds: on the 2 FDMSE VAL signers' (correct) signs, per sub-parameter quantiles of the
   ratio "distance to the natives / natives' own spread", scaled so that a target share of correct signs is
   flagged (--target-fa, default 10%).
2. False alarms: the 8 TEST signers' correct signs (never used for anything else).
3. Detection: controlled mistakes injected into test signs, one at a time - hand moved to another body zone,
   palm turned 90 degrees, a clearly different handshape, movement shrunk to a quarter, movement reversed,
   second hand dropped from a two-handed sign. Detected = the right parameter is flagged; attributed = it is
   also reported as the main problem.
Writes models/best/correction_eval.json and stores the thresholds in the reference library.
"""
import argparse, json, os, sys, time

import numpy as np

ROOT = os.path.abspath(os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), ".."))
sys.path.insert(0, ROOT); sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(ROOT, "scripts"))
from build_references import load_clip, manifest  # noqa: E402
from isl_tutor.correct import (FDMSE_ASPECT, LIBRARY, PARAM_OF, PARAMS, SUBS, Corrector, _rest_line_y,  # noqa: E402
                               _unit, canonical, streams)
from isl_tutor.features import joint_angles, interp_nan  # noqa: E402

VAL = {"s0003", "s0015"}
TEST = {"s0004", "s0006", "s0010", "s0014", "s0016", "s0017", "s0019", "s0020"}
P4 = ("handshape", "orientation", "location", "movement")


def flagged(r, thr):
    """ratios (n,6) -> (n,4) bool per parameter (a parameter fails if any of its sub-parameters does)."""
    over = r / thr
    return np.stack([np.any(over[:, [k for k, s in enumerate(SUBS) if PARAM_OF[s] == p]] > 1, 1) for p in P4], 1)


def thresholds_for(R, target):
    """per-sub thresholds = the same upper quantile of each sub's val ratios, chosen so that the share of val signs
    with ANY parameter flagged equals target."""
    lo, hi = 0.0, 0.5
    for _ in range(40):
        q = (lo + hi) / 2
        thr = np.nanquantile(R, 1 - q, axis=0)
        fa = flagged(R, thr).any(1).mean()
        lo, hi = (q, hi) if fa < target else (lo, q)
    return np.nanquantile(R, 1 - lo, axis=0)


# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- controlled mistakes
def _frame(D):
    a = _unit(D[:, 9] - D[:, 0]); n = _unit(np.cross(D[:, 5] - D[:, 0], D[:, 17] - D[:, 0]))
    return a, n, np.cross(n, a)


def _body(c):
    P = interp_nan(c["P"])
    origin = P[:, [11, 12]].mean(1)
    scale = float(np.nanmedian(np.linalg.norm(P[:, 11] - P[:, 12], axis=-1)))
    return origin, scale, (_rest_line_y(P) - np.nanmean(origin[:, 1])) / scale

"""Feature D, step 1: English sentence -> ISL gloss sequence (rule-based).

Rules (Zeshan 2003; Ghosh & Mamidi, ICON 2022 — see docs/isl-sign-tutor-research.pdf section 6):
  * time expressions first
  * subject - object - verb (verb last)
  * adjectives and numbers after the noun they modify
  * no articles, copulas or auxiliaries; verbs and nouns lemmatised (no tense, no plural -s)
  * negation at the end; WH-question word at the very end (NOT before WH)
Uses spaCy's small English model for the parse. Returns glosses in CAPS plus flags for the
non-manual markers the player should mention (question / negation).
"""
import spacy

_nlp = None
WH = {"what", "where", "when", "who", "whom", "whose", "which", "why", "how"}
TIME_WORDS = {"today", "tomorrow", "yesterday", "now", "morning", "evening", "night", "afternoon",
              "monday", "tuesday", "wednesday", "thursday", "friday", "saturday", "sunday",
              "week", "month", "year", "later", "soon", "before", "after"}
DROP_POS = {"DET", "AUX", "PUNCT", "CCONJ", "SCONJ", "PART", "ADP", "SPACE"}
KEEP_ADP = {"with", "without", "near", "under", "inside", "outside"}   # spatial words ISL does sign
Where is the bank? -> {'gloss': ['BANK', 'WHERE'], 'question': True, 'negation': False}

I will go to school tomorrow. -> {'gloss': ['TOMORROW', 'I', 'SCHOOL', 'GO'], 'question': False, 'negation': False}

She does not like red apples. -> {'gloss': ['SHE', 'APPLE', 'RED', 'LIKE', 'NOT'], 'question': False, 'negation': True}

My father is a doctor. -> {'gloss': ['MY', 'FATHER', 'DOCTOR'], 'question': False, 'negation': False}

"""ISL Sign Tutor - local demo server. Everything runs offline on the laptop CPU.

Run:   .venv\\Scripts\\python app\\server.py      then open  http://localhost:8765
The browser streams webcam frames (JPEG, ~12 fps) over a WebSocket. This server runs the same MediaPipe
keypoints as training, the FDMSE-trained recogniser (2,002 signs) and the native-signer corrector, and sends back
landmarks, recognitions and corrections. Reference signs are skeletons of native FDMSE signers (no video is shown:
FDMSE-ISL may not be redistributed).
"""
import asyncio, base64, json, os, re, sys, time

import cv2
import numpy as np
from fastapi import FastAPI, WebSocket, WebSocketDisconnect
from fastapi.responses import FileResponse, JSONResponse
from fastapi.staticfiles import StaticFiles

          "signers, no video")

       if os.path.exists(os.path.join(BEST, f"{REC_FILE}.onnx")) else None)
CORR = Corrector() if Corrector.available() else None

# skeleton of the most typical native rendition of every word (reference_library.npz, built from the train signers)
_lib = np.load(LIBRARY) if os.path.exists(LIBRARY) else None
WORDS = json.loads(str(_lib["meta"]))["words"] if _lib is not None else (REC.classes if REC else [])
ANIM, ANIM_OFF = (_lib["anim"], _lib["anim_off"]) if _lib is not None else (None, None)
WIDX = {w: i for i, w in enumerate(WORDS)}


def _norm(s):
    return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]", "", s.lower())


def _keys(w):
    """'Ample/Plenty' -> ample, plenty; 'Bike (Motorcycle)' -> bike, motorcycle; '1/One' -> 1, one."""
    base = re.sub(r"\(.*?\)", "", w)
    inner = re.findall(r"\((.*?)\)", w)
    parts = [p for p in re.split(r"[/,&]", base) if p.strip()] + [q for x in inner for q in re.split(r"[/,]", x)]
    return [k for k in (_norm(p) for p in parts) if k]


LOOKUP = {}
for _w in WORDS:                                     # first meaning wins (e.g. 'Bright (Color)' before 'Bright (Quick...)')
    for _k in [_norm(_w)] + _keys(_w):
        LOOKUP.setdefault(_k, _w)

// ISL Sign Tutor - front end. Webcam frames -> WebSocket (backend: MediaPipe + recogniser + corrector) -> overlay + results.
// Reference signs are drawn as skeletons of native FDMSE signers (body frame: origin = shoulder midpoint, unit = shoulder width).
const $ = (s) => document.querySelector(s);
let META = null, target = null;
const pct = (x, d = 1) => (x == null ? "—" : (100 * x).toFixed(d) + "%");

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- tabs
document.querySelectorAll("#tabs button").forEach((b) => b.onclick = () => {
  document.querySelectorAll("#tabs button").forEach((x) => x.classList.toggle("on", x === b));
  document.querySelectorAll(".tab").forEach((t) => t.classList.toggle("on", t.id === b.dataset.tab));
});

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- skeleton player (67 points: pose 0-24, left hand 25-45, right hand 46-66)
const HAND = [[0,1],[1,2],[2,3],[3,4],[0,5],[5,6],[6,7],[7,8],[5,9],[9,10],[10,11],[11,12],[9,13],[13,14],[14,15],[15,16],[13,17],[17,18],[18,19],[19,20],[0,17]];
const BODY = [[11,12],[11,13],[13,15],[12,14],[14,16],[11,23],[12,24],[23,24]];
function makePlayer(canvas) {
  const pl = { clips: [], i: 0, f: 0, speed: 1, mirror: true, acc: 0, last: 0, label: "" };
  const css = getComputedStyle(document.documentElement);
  function draw(fr, label) {
    const c = canvas, ctx = c.getContext("2d");
    c.width = c.clientWidth * devicePixelRatio; c.height = c.clientHeight * devicePixelRatio;
    ctx.clearRect(0, 0, c.width, c.height);
    if (!fr) return;
    const s = c.height / 4.4, cx = c.width / 2, cy = c.height * 0.36, m = pl.mirror ? -1 : 1;
    const P = (p) => [cx + m * p[0] * s, cy + p[1] * s];
    const seen = (k0) => fr.slice(k0, k0 + 21).some((p) => p[0] !== 0 || p[1] !== 0);
    const seg = (a, b, col, w) => { const [x1, y1] = P(fr[a]), [x2, y2] = P(fr[b]); ctx.strokeStyle = col; ctx.lineWidth = w;
      ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(x1, y1); ctx.lineTo(x2, y2); ctx.stroke(); };
    ctx.lineCap = "round";
    BODY.forEach(([a, b]) => seg(a, b, css.getPropertyValue("--muted"), 4 * devicePixelRatio));
    const [hx, hy] = P(fr[0]); ctx.strokeStyle = css.getPropertyValue("--muted"); ctx.lineWidth = 3 * devicePixelRatio;
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(hx, hy, 0.38 * s, 0, 2 * Math.PI); ctx.stroke();
    if (seen(25)) HAND.forEach(([a, b]) => seg(25 + a, 25 + b, "#ffa94d", 3 * devicePixelRatio));
    if (seen(46)) HAND.forEach(([a, b]) => seg(46 + a, 46 + b, "#4dabf7", 3 * devicePixelRatio));
    if (label) { ctx.fillStyle = css.getPropertyValue("--ink"); ctx.font = `600 ${22 * devicePixelRatio}px Segoe UI, sans-serif`;
      ctx.fillText(label, 14 * devicePixelRatio, 32 * devicePixelRatio); }
  }
  function tick(t) {
    const clip = pl.clips[pl.i];
    if (clip) {
      pl.acc += Math.min(200, t - (pl.last || t)) * pl.speed; pl.last = t;
      while (pl.acc >= 1000 / 12) { pl.acc -= 1000 / 12; pl.f++;
        if (pl.f >= clip.frames.length + 8) { pl.f = 0; pl.i = (pl.i + 1) % pl.clips.length; } }
      const c2 = pl.clips[pl.i];
      draw(c2.frames[Math.min(pl.f, c2.frames.length - 1)], c2.label);
    }
    requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  pl.load = (clips) => { pl.clips = clips.filter((c) => c.frames && c.frames.length); pl.i = 0; pl.f = 0; };
  pl.restart = () => { pl.i = 0; pl.f = 0; };
  requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  return pl;
}
const refPlayer = makePlayer($("#refCanvas")), showPlayer = makePlayer($("#showCanvas"));

  META = await (await fetch("/api/meta")).json();
  const st = $("#status");
  st.textContent = META.model_ready ? `ready · ${META.classes} signs · corrections for ${META.vocabulary.filter((v) => v.can_correct).length} words · all offline`

    return [k for k in (_norm(p) for p in re.split(r"[/,&]", re.sub(r"\\(.*?\\)", "", w))) if k]

    return None
'''

ROOT = sys.argv[1]

 ('        self.thresh = np.array(meta.get("thresholds", [2.0] * len(SUBS)), np.float32)\n',
  '        self.thresh = np.array(meta.get("thresholds", [2.0] * len(SUBS)), np.float32)          # "tip" level\n'
  '        self.thresh_fix = np.array(meta.get("thresholds_fix", self.thresh * 1.3), np.float32)  # "fix this" level\n'),
 ('''        over = r / self.thresh                                   # > 1 = flagged
        par = {}
        for p in ("handshape", "orientation", "location", "movement"):
            ks = [k for k, sub in enumerate(SUBS) if PARAM_OF[sub] == p]
            par[p] = dict(score=round(float(max(over[ks])), 2), ok=bool(max(over[ks]) <= 1.0))
        two_ref = self.two[w, :self.n_nat[w]].mean()
        hands_wrong = (two_ref >= 0.7 and not s["two"]) or (two_ref <= 0.3 and s["two"])
        par["hands"] = dict(score=1.5 if hands_wrong else 0.0, ok=not hands_wrong)
''',
  '''        over, over_fix = r / self.thresh, r / self.thresh_fix     # > 1 = beyond the natives' own variation
        par = {}
        for p in ("handshape", "orientation", "location", "movement"):
            ks = [k for k, sub in enumerate(SUBS) if PARAM_OF[sub] == p]
            lvl = "fix" if max(over_fix[ks]) > 1 else "tip" if max(over[ks]) > 1 else "ok"
            par[p] = dict(score=round(float(max(over[ks])), 2), level=lvl, ok=lvl == "ok")
        two_ref = self.two[w, :self.n_nat[w]].mean()
        hands_wrong = (two_ref >= 0.7 and not s["two"]) or (two_ref <= 0.3 and s["two"])
        par["hands"] = dict(score=9.0 if hands_wrong else 0.0, level="fix" if hands_wrong else "ok", ok=not hands_wrong)
'''),
 ('''        bad = [p for p in PARAMS if not par[p]["ok"]]
        worst = max(bad, key=lambda p: par[p]["score"]) if bad else None
        return {"ok": not bad, "word": word, "parameters": par, "worst": worst, "nearest_native": near,
                "message": "Matches the native signers." if not bad else
                f"{worst.capitalize()}: {par[worst]['fix']}."}''',
  '''        fixes = [p for p in PARAMS if par[p]["level"] == "fix"]
        tips = [p for p in PARAMS if par[p]["level"] == "tip"]
        worst = max(fixes or tips, key=lambda p: par[p]["score"]) if (fixes or tips) else None
        level = "fix" if fixes else "tip" if tips else "ok"
        msg = ("Matches the native signers." if level == "ok" else
               f"Fix the {worst}: {par[worst]['fix']}." if level == "fix" else
               f"Good. Tip for the {worst}: {par[worst]['fix']}.")
        return {"ok": level != "fix", "level": level, "word": word, "parameters": par, "worst": worst,
                "nearest_native": near, "message": msg}'''),

"""End-to-end test of the demo server without a webcam: stream real FDMSE test-signer videos (12 fps, 640 px wide)
through the WebSocket exactly like the browser does, in practice mode with the right target word."""
import asyncio, base64, csv, json, os, sys, time, urllib.parse

import cv2
import websockets

ROOT = sys.argv[1]
N = int(sys.argv[2]) if len(sys.argv) > 2 else 6
meta = list(csv.DictReader(open(os.path.join(ROOT, "data", "Raw", "FDMSE-ISL", "data_meta", "metadata.csv"), encoding="utf-8")))
test = [r for r in meta if r["split"] == "test"]
picks = test[:: max(1, len(test) // N)][:N]


def find_video(rel):
    for base in (os.path.join(ROOT, "data", "Raw", "FDMSE-ISL"), os.path.join(ROOT, "data", "Raw", "FDMSE-ISL", "data")):
        p = os.path.join(base, rel)
        if os.path.exists(p):
            return p
    return None


async def run(row):
    path = find_video(row["video_dir"])
    if path is None:
        return print("video not found:", row["video_dir"])
    word = row["class"]
    url = "ws://127.0.0.1:8765/ws?" + urllib.parse.urlencode({"mode": "practice", "target": word})
    cap = cv2.VideoCapture(path)
    fps = cap.get(cv2.CAP_PROP_FPS) or 60
    step = max(1, round(fps / 12))
    frames, i = [], 0
    while True:
        ok, fr = cap.read()
        if not ok:
            break
        if i % step == 0:
            h, w = fr.shape[:2]
            frames.append(cv2.resize(fr, (640, round(640 * h / w))))
        i += 1
    frames += [frames[-1]] * 10                       # the signer rests at the end -> the attempt is closed
    results, t0 = [], time.perf_counter()
    async with websockets.connect(url, max_size=2 ** 24) as ws:
        for k, fr in enumerate(frames):
            jpg = base64.b64encode(cv2.imencode(".jpg", fr, [cv2.IMWRITE_JPEG_QUALITY, 70])[1]).decode()
            await ws.send(json.dumps({"type": "frame", "ts": int(k * 1000 / 12), "jpg": "data:image/jpeg;base64," + jpg}))
            while True:                               # one frame in flight, like the browser
                m = json.loads(await ws.recv())
                if m["type"] == "frame":
                    break
                results.append(m)
        try:
            while True:
                results.append(json.loads(await asyncio.wait_for(ws.recv(), 3)))
        except (asyncio.TimeoutError, websockets.ConnectionClosed):
            pass
    dt = time.perf_counter() - t0
    res = [r for r in results if r.get("type") == "result"]
    print(f"\n{os.path.basename(path)}  target '{word}'  ({len(frames)} frames, {dt / len(frames) * 1000:.0f} ms/frame round trip)")
    for r in res:
… 14 more lines not shown

import sys

rep = [
 ('''def err_location(c):
    origin, scale, rest = _body(c)
    D = c["D"]; y = np.nanmedian((D[:, 0, 1] - origin[:, 1]) / scale)
    dy = 0.75 if y + 0.75 < rest - 0.15 else -0.75                  # move a zone down if possible, else up
    D = D.copy(); D[..., 1] += dy * scale
    return dict(c, D=D)
''',
 '''def _span_weight(c, ramp=2):
    """1 while the dominant hand is raised (the sign itself), ramping to 0 over `ramp` frames, 0 at rest - so a
    mistake is made only while signing, as a learner would (the resting hands are left where they are)."""
    origin, scale, rest = _body(c)
    D = c["D"]; seen = ~np.isnan(D[:, 0, 0])
    y = (interp_nan(D[:, 0, :2].copy())[:, 1] - origin[:, 1]) / scale
    idx = np.flatnonzero(seen & (y < rest))
    w = np.zeros(len(D), np.float32)
    if len(idx) == 0:
        return w, origin, scale, rest
    s, e = idx[0], idx[-1]
    w[s:e + 1] = 1
    for k in range(1, ramp + 1):
        w[max(0, s - k)] = max(w[max(0, s - k)], 1 - k / (ramp + 1))
        w[min(len(w) - 1, e + k)] = max(w[min(len(w) - 1, e + k)], 1 - k / (ramp + 1))
    return w, origin, scale, rest


def err_location(c):
    w, origin, scale, rest = _span_weight(c)
    D = c["D"]; y = np.nanmedian(((D[:, 0, 1] - origin[:, 1]) / scale)[w == 1])
    dy = 0.75 if y + 0.75 < rest - 0.15 else -0.75                  # move a zone down if possible, else up
    D = D.copy(); D[..., 1] += (dy * scale * w)[:, None]
    return dict(c, D=D)
'''),
 ('''def err_shrink(c, s=0.25):
    D = c["D"].copy(); w = _wrist_path(c); m = np.nanmean(w, 0)
    D[..., :2] -= ((1 - s) * (w - m))[:, None]
    return dict(c, D=D)


def err_reverse(c):
    D = c["D"].copy(); w = _wrist_path(c)
    D[..., :2] += (w[::-1] - w)[:, None]
    return dict(c, D=D)
''',
 '''def err_shrink(c, s=0.25):
    wt = _span_weight(c)[0]
    D = c["D"].copy(); w = _wrist_path(c); m = np.nanmean(w[wt == 1], 0)
    D[..., :2] -= ((1 - s) * (w - m) * wt[:, None])[:, None]
    return dict(c, D=D)

    wt = _span_weight(c)[0]

    idx = np.flatnonzero(wt == 1)
    if len(idx) < 2:

--- old
    loc = _resample(loc)
    traj = loc - loc.mean(0)
+++ new
    # movement = the stroke: the middle 70% of the raised span, without the approach from / return to rest
    k = int(round(0.15 * len(loc)))
    stroke = _resample(loc[k:len(loc) - k] if len(loc) - 2 * k >= 3 else loc)
    traj = stroke - stroke.mean(0)
    loc = _resample(loc)

Caught: handshape 97.7%, second hand 100%, location 87.0% (named first 80%), movement reversed 78.7%, shrunk 73.8%, palm 90deg 71.0%. WebSocket end-to-end test with FDMSE test videos: 6/6 recognised, 5/6 'matches'; about 110 ms/frame round trip (MediaPipe is the bottleneck, about 8-9 fps). tasks.md updated (Phase 8 ticked, Phase 9 added). memory ok
Shell cwd was reset to e:\AIDS\Project

The correction part is built, measured and wired into the demo. It hasn't yet been checked on real learners' mistakes; that needs an ISL teacher.

How it judges your sign: it compares your attempt with all 10 native signers of that word (from the FDMSE training signers). It checks:

  • handshape;
  • orientation: which way the palm faces and where the fingers point;
  • location on the body;
  • movement: size and path;
  • one or two hands.

Something is flagged only if your sign differs from the natives clearly more than they differ from each other. Left-handed signers are mirrored, so they're judged fairly. Feedback has two levels: a tip for small differences, and "fix this" only for clear ones. Each sign takes about 10 ms to check.

How well it works, measured on the 8 test signers it never saw (thresholds tuned only on the 2 validation signers):

  • Correct signs: 9.5% of correct native signs are told to fix something (16.2% get at least a tip).
  • Deliberate mistakes: I injected these one at a time into real test signs:
Mistake Caught as "fix this" Named as the main problem
different handshape 97.7% 93.4%
second hand left out 100% 100%
hand at the wrong body location 87.0% 80.2%
movement reversed 78.7% 69.4%
movement too small 73.8% 68.5%
palm turned 90° 71.0% 67.8%

Orientation and movement are the weakest. Webcam keypoints give only a rough sense of depth, so a palm rotation is harder to see than a handshape.

What changed in the demo (app/server.py, app/web/):

  • Practice: 2,002 searchable words. Each has an animated skeleton of the most typical native signer, with mirror and slow-motion buttons, plus five feedback tiles: green ✔, amber tip, red fix.
  • Show me: English is put into ISL word order and played as native skeletons. Two-word signs such as "Thank You" stay together, and unknown words such as names are fingerspelled with FDMSE's own A–Z letter signs. No FDMSE video is shown anywhere.
  • Results tab: the recognition comparison with HWGAT and the correction evaluation.

Tested end to end without a webcam: I streamed six real test-signer videos frame by frame through the same connection the browser uses. All six were recognised correctly. Five were judged to match the natives; the sixth got a handshape note, one of the expected false alarms on correct signs. On this laptop the hand tracking is the slow part (about 8–9 frames per second); recognising and checking a finished sign takes about 20 ms.

tasks.md is updated: Phase 8 is ticked off, and a new Phase 9 lists what's done and what's left.

What I need from you:

  1. Try it with your webcam: double-click E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\run_demo.cmd and allow camera access. Pick a word in Practice, watch the skeleton, sign it and lower your hands. Tell me how the feedback feels, especially any message that seems wrong or confusing.
  2. Delete isl-sign-tutor-data from Kaggle, as the licence requires. Everything needed is on your laptop.
  3. Find an ISL teacher if you can. These numbers come from native signers and simulated mistakes. Whether the feedback is right for real learners' mistakes is the main open question, and the one your professor is most likely to ask.

Phase 6 · Testing as a learner

Native videos, a self-test mode, and a step-by-step test guide for a laptop camera.

Bash: Compute per-word test accuracy of the recogniser
input
.venv/Scripts/python.exe - <<'EOF' 2>&1 | grep -v Warning
import json, pickle, sys, numpy as np, onnxruntime as ort
sys.path.insert(0, ".")
from isl_tutor.data import features, resample
M = "models/best/isl_outputs"
meta = json.load(open(f"{M}/recognizer_meta.json")); T = meta["T"]; classes = meta["classes"]; cid = {w: i for i, w in enumerate(classes)}
d = pickle.load(open("kaggle/dataset/labelled.pkl", "rb")); test = d["sets"]["test"]; del d
X = np.stack([features(resample(it["pts"], T), None) for it in test]).astype(np.float32)
y = np.array([cid[it["word"]] for it in test])
so = ort.SessionOptions(); so.intra_op_num_threads = 4
s = ort.InferenceSession(f"{M}/recognizer.onnx", so, providers=["CPUExecutionProvider"])
pred = np.concatenate([s.run(None, {"x": X[i:i + 512]})[0].argmax(1) for i in range(0, len(X), 512)])
acc = {w: float((pred[y == i] == i).mean()) for w, i in cid.items() if (y == i).any()}
json.dump(acc, open(f"{M}/per_word_test_accuracy.json", "w"), indent=0)
v = np.array(list(acc.values()))
print(f"per-word test top-1 over {len(acc)} words: mean {v.mean():.3f}; words at 100%: {(v == 1).sum()}; below 75%: {(v < 0.75).sum()}")
EOF

         "Car", "Bus", "Train", "Hot", "Cold", "Big", "Small", "Want", "Like", "I", "You", "My", "Your", "Her/She", "He/Him",
         "Red", "Apple", "Market", "Office", "Home", "Learn", "Sign", "Deaf", "Hear", "Speak", "Fine", "How", "Who", "When", "Why"]

rows = []
for c in cands:
    w = idx.get(c.lower()) or next((x for x in C.words if x.lower().split("/")[0] == c.lower()), None)
    if not w:
        print("not in vocabulary:", c); continue
    k = C.index[w]; n = C.n_nat[k]
    two = float(C.two[k, :n].mean())
    traj = C.nat["traj"][k, :n]; rms = float(np.nanmedian(np.sqrt((traj ** 2).sum(-1).mean(-1))))
    net = float(np.nanmedian(np.linalg.norm(traj[:, -1] - traj[:, 0], axis=-1)))
    y = float(np.nanmedian(C.nat["loc"][k, :n, N // 3:2 * N // 3, 1]))
    rows.append((w, acc.get(w, 0), two, rms, net, y))
print(f"{'word':16s} {'test acc':>8s} {'2-hand':>6s} {'move':>5s} {'net':>5s}  zone")
for w, a, two, rms, net, y in sorted(rows, key=lambda r: (-r[1], r[0])):
    print(f"{w:16s} {a:8.0%} {two:6.0%} {rms:5.2f} {net:5.2f}  {_zone(y)}")

--- old
    def __init__(self, max_seconds=8):
        self.buf = deque(maxlen=FPS * max_seconds)

    def push(self, pose, lh, rh, face):
        self.buf.append((pose, lh, rh, face))
        if len(self.buf) < int(0.8 * FPS):
            return None, False
        P, L, R, Fc = (np.stack(z) for z in zip(*self.buf))
        segs = sign_segments(P, L, R, FPS)
        signing = bool(segs) and segs[-1][1] >= len(P) - 2
        if segs and segs[-1][1] < len(P) - int(0.3 * FPS):           # last segment followed by a rest
            s, e = segs[-1]
            self.buf.clear()
            return (P[s:e + 1], L[s:e + 1], R[s:e + 1], Fc[s:e + 1]), False
        return None, signing

    def reset(self):
        self.buf.clear()
+++ new
    def __init__(self, max_seconds=8):
        self.buf = deque(maxlen=FPS * max_seconds)
        self.ts = deque(maxlen=FPS * max_seconds)
        self.last_span = None                        # (first, last) frame timestamp of the last finished attempt

    def push(self, pose, lh, rh, face, ts=None):
        self.buf.append((pose, lh, rh, face)); self.ts.append(ts)
        if len(self.buf) < int(0.8 * FPS):
            return None, False
        P, L, R, Fc = (np.stack(z) for z in zip(*self.buf))
        segs = sign_segments(P, L, R, FPS)
        signing = bool(segs) and segs[-1][1] >= len(P) - 2
        if segs and segs[-1][1] < len(P) - int(0.3 * FPS):           # last segment followed by a rest
            s, e = segs[-1]
            self.last_span = (self.ts[s], self.ts[e])
            self.buf.clear(); self.ts.clear()
            return (P[s:e + 1], L[s:e + 1], R[s:e + 1], Fc[s:e + 1]), False
        return None, signing

    def reset(self):
        self.buf.clear(); self.ts.clear()

    <div class="card"><h3>✎ Correct</h3><p>Your sign is time-aligned to the native signers of that word and judged parameter by parameter against how much natives themselves vary; replay your attempt next to the native video.</p></div>

    <div class="card"><h3>▶ Show me</h3><p>Type English; it is reordered into ISL grammar and native signers' videos play word by word, fingerspelling words outside the vocabulary.</p></div>
  </div>
  <h3 class="sec">How does it compare?</h3>
  <p class="muted" id="vsnote">Isolated-sign recognition accuracy. General chatbots: zero-shot on 300 ASL signs (Javorek et al., 2026) — they sample video at about 1 frame per second and cannot measure hand angles.
    HWGAT: the published state of the art on this dataset, run through the same pipeline on the same 8 test signers.</p>
  <div class="bars" id="vsbars"></div>
</section>

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- native videos: play only the sign (start..end), looped or once
function watchSeg(v) {
  v.addEventListener("timeupdate", () => { const c = v._seg; if (!c || c.end == null) return;
    if (v.currentTime >= c.end) { if (v._loop) v.currentTime = c.start || 0; else { v.pause(); const f = v._onEnd; v._onEnd = null; if (f) f(); } } });
  v.addEventListener("ended", () => { if (v._loop) { v.currentTime = (v._seg || {}).start || 0; v.play().catch(() => {}); }
    else { const f = v._onEnd; v._onEnd = null; if (f) f(); } });
}
function segPlay(v, clip, rate = 1, loop = true, onEnd = null) {
  v._seg = clip; v._loop = loop; v._onEnd = onEnd;
  const go = () => { v.currentTime = clip.start || 0; v.playbackRate = rate; v.play().catch(() => {}); };
  if (v.getAttribute("src") !== clip.url) { v.onloadedmetadata = go; v.src = clip.url; } else go();
}
["#refVid", "#cmpVid", "#trVid", "#showVid"].forEach((s) => watchSeg($(s)));
const NAT = {};                                          // word -> /api/natives answer (cached)
async function natives(word) {
  if (!NAT[word]) { const r = await fetch("/api/natives/" + encodeURIComponent(word)); NAT[word] = r.ok ? await r.json() : null; }
  return NAT[word];
}
const firstClip = (n) => (n && n.natives.length ? n.natives[0].views.front || Object.values(n.natives[0].views)[0] : null);

function makePlayer(canvas) {
  const pl = { clips: [], i: 0, f: 0, speed: 1, mirror: true, acc: 0, last: 0 };
  const css = getComputedStyle(document.documentElement);
  function draw(fr, label) {
    const c = canvas, ctx = c.getContext("2d");
    if (!c.clientWidth) return;
    c.width = c.clientWidth * devicePixelRatio; c.height = c.clientHeight * devicePixelRatio;
    ctx.clearRect(0, 0, c.width, c.height);
    if (!fr) return;
    const s = c.height / 4.4, cx = c.width / 2, cy = c.height * 0.36, m = pl.mirror ? -1 : 1;
    const P = (p) => [cx + m * p[0] * s, cy + p[1] * s];
    const seen = (k0) => fr.slice(k0, k0 + 21).some((p) => p[0] !== 0 || p[1] !== 0);
    const seg = (a, b, col, w) => { const [x1, y1] = P(fr[a]), [x2, y2] = P(fr[b]); ctx.strokeStyle = col; ctx.lineWidth = w;
      ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(x1, y1); ctx.lineTo(x2, y2); ctx.stroke(); };
    ctx.lineCap = "round";
    BODY.forEach(([a, b]) => seg(a, b, css.getPropertyValue("--muted"), 4 * devicePixelRatio));
    const [hx, hy] = P(fr[0]); ctx.strokeStyle = css.getPropertyValue("--muted"); ctx.lineWidth = 3 * devicePixelRatio;
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.arc(hx, hy, 0.38 * s, 0, 2 * Math.PI); ctx.stroke();
    if (seen(25)) HAND.forEach(([a, b]) => seg(25 + a, 25 + b, "#ffa94d", 3 * devicePixelRatio));
    if (seen(46)) HAND.forEach(([a, b]) => seg(46 + a, 46 + b, "#4dabf7", 3 * devicePixelRatio));
    if (label) { ctx.fillStyle = css.getPropertyValue("--ink"); ctx.font = `600 ${22 * devicePixelRatio}px Segoe UI, sans-serif`;
      ctx.fillText(label, 14 * devicePixelRatio, 32 * devicePixelRatio); }
  }

B = "http://127.0.0.1:8765"

async def go():
    cap = cv2.VideoCapture(path); fr, i = [], 0
    while True:
        ok, f = cap.read()
        if not ok: break
        if i % 5 == 0: fr.append(cv2.resize(f, (640, 507)))

    fr += [fr[-1]] * 10

            while True:
                m = json.loads(await ws.recv())
                if m["type"] == "frame": break

# Testing the tutor yourself (no ISL teacher needed)

You will be the learner. The native signers' videos show what is correct. You will also make some mistakes **on purpose**, so the right answer is always known: either you copied the native signer as well as you can, or you made one specific mistake. The app logs every attempt and calculates the numbers for you.

**What this measures**

| # | Question | Measured by |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Does it recognise *your* signs on *your* webcam? | Part A: careful attempts |
| 2 | Does it leave correct signs alone? | Part A: how often careful attempts are told to "fix" something |
| 3 | Does it catch real mistakes and name the right one? | Part B: deliberate mistakes |
| 4 | Does it translate signed sentences? | Part C: 12 sentences |

Time: about 1 hour for Parts A + B and 20 minutes for Part C. You can split it over several sittings, because every attempt is saved as you go.

---

## 1. Before you start

1. **Plug in the laptop** and close heavy apps (tracking needs the CPU).
2. Double-click **`run_demo.cmd`**. It opens http://localhost:8765 in your browser; use Chrome or Edge, and allow the camera.
3. **Camera framing:** sit about 1–1.5 m from the laptop, so the picture shows your head down to your waist. Your hands must stay in the picture when raised to your face and when held out to the sides.
4. **Light and background:** light from the front (a window or lamp behind the laptop, not behind you), a plain background, and no other person in view.
5. **Clothes:** plain long sleeves or short sleeves are fine. Avoid gloves, rings and bracelets that cover the fingers.
6. **Hands rest = sign finished.** Start each sign with your hands resting low (lap or table). Raise them, sign, then **put them down again**. The tutor only judges the sign once your hands are back down.
7. If you are left-handed, sign with your left hand as the main hand. The tutor mirrors left-handed signers automatically.

## 2. How to learn a sign (you don't need to know ISL)

In the **Practice** tab, tick **"only the 12 test words"** and click a word. Then:

1. Watch the **native signer** video (★ = the most typical of the 10 signers) at **0.5×**. Keep **Mirror: on**, so you can copy it like a mirror.
2. Click **Left side** and **Right side**. The side views show which way the palm faces and how far the hands move forwards; these are hard to see from the front.
3. Switch between **native signers 1–10** to see what varies and what stays the same. Copy what they all share.
4. Practise 2–3 times **without** test mode. After each attempt, the **replay** shows you (left) next to the native signer (right) at the same speed. Use it to check yourself.

---

## 3. Part A: careful attempts (recognition and false alarms)

In Practice, tick **Test mode** and set **Next attempt** to **"Careful — copy the native signer as exactly as I can"**.

For each of the 12 test words, do **2 careful attempts** (24 in total). After each attempt:

* watch the replay;
* click **"Yes — log it"** if you copied the sign as well as you could;
* click **"No — don't count it"** if something went wrong that wasn't about the sign (you sneezed, left the picture, signed the wrong word).

| # | Word | Hands | Where | Movement | ☐ careful 1 | ☐ careful 2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Hello | one | at the chin | small | ☐ | ☐ |
| 2 | Mother | one | at the chin | small | ☐ | ☐ |
| 3 | Water | one | at the face | medium | ☐ | ☐ |
| 4 | Come | one | at the chest | large | ☐ | ☐ |
| 5 | Bad | one | at the chest | large | ☐ | ☐ |
| 6 | Name | one | at the chest | medium | ☐ | ☐ |
| 7 | Sorry | one | at the chest | small | ☐ | ☐ |
| 8 | Thank You | two | at the chest | small | ☐ | ☐ |
| 9 | Family | two | at the chest | medium | ☐ | ☐ |
| 10 | School | two | at the chest | medium | ☐ | ☐ |

Written for: you, as the tester at the webcam, with no ISL knowledge assumed.

Yes, you can be the learner. TESTING.md sets up the test so that you always know the right answer: you either copy the native signer as well as you can, or you make one specific mistake on purpose. The app logs every attempt and works out the numbers for you.

A correction on the videos: I said earlier the licence didn't allow showing FDMSE videos. It forbids redistributing, sharing or hosting them; playing your own licensed copy on your own laptop for an academic project is fine. So the app now plays them, but only from your local copy and only on this laptop. Don't screen-record them for sharing; for any public recording there's a Skeleton button.

What's new in the app:

  • Practice:
    • Native videos: the real video with front, left-side and right-side views, any of the 10 native signers (★ marks the most typical), 1×, 0.5× or 0.25× speed, mirror, and looping over just the sign. The side views make palm direction easy to see.
    • Replay: after each attempt, your own attempt plays next to the native signer at the same speed, so you can check the tutor's feedback with your own eyes.
    • Test mode: choose what you're about to do (careful, or one of six deliberate mistakes), then confirm with "Yes, log it" or "No, don't count it".
  • Translate: each recognised sign shows the native video of the word the tutor understood. In test mode you pick the sentence you're signing and judge whether the English meaning is right.
  • Show me: the sentence plays as real native videos, one sign after another.
  • Results tab: your self-test summary next to the native-signer numbers.

I tested every part (videos, seeking, the replay timing, logging and the summary) with a real test video sent through the live connection. I haven't seen the page in a browser, though, so your session will be its first real look.

What TESTING.md asks you to do (about 1 hour 20 minutes, which you can split over several sittings):

Part What you do What it measures
A 12 test words × 2 careful attempts (24) recognition on your webcam, and how often correct-looking signs get a "fix"
B 54 deliberate mistakes: wrong location (about 20 cm higher or lower), palm turned a quarter turn, fist ↔ flat hand, movement a quarter as big, movement reversed, one hand only whether each mistake is caught and named correctly
C 12 short sentences (23 words to learn first) translation: wrong signs per sentence, whole sentence right, English meaning right

All 12 test words were recognised 100% of the time for the native test signers. So if one fails for you, it's about your signing or your camera, not the model's weak spot.

One honest limit: you're a beginner, so a "fix" on a careful attempt may be a real mistake of yours. TESTING.md says to report that number as "flagged careful attempts", not "false alarms".

To start, double-click E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\run_demo.cmd and open E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\TESTING.md beside it. Send me the Results tab numbers when you've done a part.

"""Drive headless Chrome (fake camera) through the demo and take screenshots of each tab."""
import asyncio, base64, json, os, subprocess, sys, tempfile, time, urllib.request

import websockets

CHROME = r"C:\Program Files\Google\Chrome\Application\chrome.exe"
OUT = sys.argv[1]
os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
prof = tempfile.mkdtemp()
proc = subprocess.Popen([CHROME, "--headless=new", "--remote-debugging-port=9333", f"--user-data-dir={prof}",
                         "--window-size=1440,1150", "--autoplay-policy=no-user-gesture-required",
                         "--use-fake-ui-for-media-stream", "--use-fake-device-for-media-stream", "about:blank"])
for _ in range(50):
    try:
        tabs = json.load(urllib.request.urlopen("http://127.0.0.1:9333/json", timeout=2)); break
    except Exception:
        time.sleep(0.3)
ws_url = next(t["webSocketDebuggerUrl"] for t in tabs if t["type"] == "page")


async def main():
    async with websockets.connect(ws_url, max_size=2 ** 26) as ws:
        n = [0]

        async def cmd(method, **params):
            n[0] += 1; i = n[0]
            await ws.send(json.dumps({"id": i, "method": method, "params": params}))
            while True:
                m = json.loads(await ws.recv())
                if m.get("id") == i:
                    return m.get("result", m.get("error"))
                if m.get("method") == "Runtime.consoleAPICalled" and m["params"]["type"] in ("error", "warning"):
                    print("  console:", " ".join(str(a.get("value", a.get("description", ""))) for a in m["params"]["args"])[:200])
                if m.get("method") == "Runtime.exceptionThrown":
                    print("  JS EXCEPTION:", m["params"]["exceptionDetails"].get("exception", {}).get("description", "")[:300])

        async def js(code):
            r = await cmd("Runtime.evaluate", expression=code, awaitPromise=True, returnByValue=True)
            return (r or {}).get("result", {}).get("value")

        async def shot(name, wait):
            await asyncio.sleep(wait)
            await js("0")                                   # drain console events
            r = await cmd("Page.captureScreenshot", format="png")
            open(os.path.join(OUT, name + ".png"), "wb").write(base64.b64decode(r["data"]))
            print("saved", name)

        await cmd("Page.enable"); await cmd("Runtime.enable")
        await cmd("Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride", width=1440, height=1150, deviceScaleFactor=1, mobile=False)
        await cmd("Page.navigate", url="http://localhost:8765")
        await shot("1_overview", 5)
        await js("document.querySelector('[data-tab=practice]').click(); document.querySelector('#onlyTest').click(); pickWord('Thank You')")
        await shot("2_practice_word", 6)
        print("  ref video:", await js("(()=>{const v=document.querySelector('#refVid');return {src:v.src.slice(-40),t:v.currentTime.toFixed(2),rate:v.playbackRate,paused:v.paused,ready:v.readyState,w:v.videoWidth}})()"))
        await js("document.querySelectorAll('#viewBtns button')[1].click()")
        await shot("3_practice_leftview", 4)
        await js("document.querySelector('#testMode').click(); document.querySelector('#startP').click()")
        await shot("4_practice_camera", 7)
        print("  camera:", await js("(()=>({state:document.querySelector('#stateP').textContent, frames: camP.frames.length, ws: camP.ws && camP.ws.readyState}))()"))
        await js("camP.stop(); document.querySelector('[data-tab=translate]').click()")

# Testing the tutor yourself: step by step

**For:** you, using the laptop's built-in front camera. No ISL knowledge is needed: the native signers' videos teach you each sign.

**The idea.** You are the learner. For every attempt, you decide in advance what you will do: either **copy the native signer as well as you can**, or make **one specific mistake on purpose** (for example, sign at the wrong height). So the right answer is always known, and the app checks whether the tutor noticed. Every attempt is saved, and the app calculates the numbers.

**What you will measure**

| # | Question | Part |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Does it recognise *my* signs on *my* laptop camera? | A |
| 2 | Does it leave my careful signs alone? | A |
| 3 | Does it catch real mistakes and name the right one? | B |
| 4 | Does it translate short signed sentences? | C |

**Time plan.** Three sittings. You can stop at any point, because every logged attempt is saved immediately.

| Sitting | What | Time |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Setup + learn the 12 test words + Part A (24 careful attempts) | 45–60 min |
| 2 | Part B (54 deliberate mistakes on the same 12 words) | 40–50 min |
| 3 | Learn 23 sentence words + Part C (12 sentences) | 45–60 min |

---

## 1. Setup (do this at the start of every sitting)

### 1.1 Prepare the laptop
1. **Plug the laptop into power.** On battery, the processor slows to about half speed and tracking lags.
2. **Close every app that can use the camera:** Zoom, Teams, Google Meet tabs, the Windows Camera app. Only one app can use the camera at a time.
3. Close other heavy programs (games, video editors, many browser tabs).

### 1.2 Start the tutor
1. Open `E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor` and **double-click `run_demo.cmd`**.
2. A **black console window** opens. **Leave it open the whole time**: it *is* the tutor. Closing it stops everything. You can minimise it.
3. Within about 10 seconds the browser opens **http://localhost:8765**. If it doesn't, open Chrome or Edge yourself and type that address.
4. Wait until the **Overview** page shows a blue label starting with **"ready · 2002 signs"**.

### 1.3 Position yourself (laptop camera)
A laptop camera sits low and close, so arrange it like this:

1. Put the laptop on a table. **Raise it** with a few thick books or a box, so the **camera (the small dot above the screen) is roughly at your chin height** when you sit.
2. Sit on a chair **about 80–100 cm from the screen**: roughly one arm's length plus a hand. You must still be able to read the screen.
3. **Tilt the screen** until the camera picture (in step 1.4) shows you **from the top of your head down to your belly button**, with some space on both sides.
4. **Light:** face a window or a lamp, with the light coming from behind the laptop towards you. **Don't sit with a bright window behind you**, because your hands become dark shapes.
5. **Background:** a plain wall is best. No other person in the picture.
6. Wear something plain. Remove rings, big bracelets and watches if they cover your fingers.

### 1.4 Camera check (4 points)
1. Click the **Practice** tab, then **Start camera**.
2. The browser asks to use the camera. Click **Allow** (choose "Allow on every visit" if offered).
3. The small label at the top left of the camera picture changes from **"starting…"** to **"tracking · NN ms/frame"** within about 3–10 seconds. The first time each sitting can take a little longer.
4. Now check four things:
   * **a. Hands resting in your lap:** white lines follow your shoulders and arms. The label says **"tracking"**.
   * **b. Both hands raised to your face, palms towards the camera, fingers spread:** an **orange** skeleton appears on one hand and a **blue** one on the other, following your fingers. The label says **"signing…"**.
   * **c. Hands held out to the sides at shoulder height:** both hand skeletons stay visible. If a hand leaves the picture, sit a little further back or tilt the screen.
   * **d. Hands back in your lap:** within about a second, the label returns to **"tracking"**.
5. Look at the **ms/frame** number while your hands are raised. **Below about 150** is fine. If it is much higher, check the power cable and close other apps.

### 1.5 One trial run (proves everything works)

Yes, you can test it with your laptop's front camera: to the browser and the tutor it's the same as a webcam. The full step-by-step guide is in E:\AIDS\Project\isl-sign-tutor\TESTING.md; keep it open beside the browser while you test.

I also checked the app in a browser for the first time. With the browser's fake camera, tracking ran at about 12 frames per second, the native videos played, and Show me stepped through its signs. I fixed two layout bugs: a skeleton showing under the video when it should be hidden, and stretched checkboxes. Your real camera, and the replay of a real attempt, are still untested; your first sitting will cover that.

What changes for a laptop camera:

  • Raise the laptop on books so the camera is about at your chin height.
  • Sit 80–100 cm away and tilt the screen until it shows you from the top of your head to your belly button.
  • Have the light in front of you, not a window behind you.
  • Close Zoom, Teams and the Camera app first; only one app can use the camera at a time.

The process:

  1. Setup (each sitting).
    • Plug in the laptop and double-click run_demo.cmd.
    • Leave the black window open; closing it stops the tutor.
    • In the browser, go to Practice → Start camera → Allow.
  2. Camera check:
    • Hands in your lap: the label says "tracking".
    • Hands raised: orange and blue skeletons follow your fingers, and the label says "signing…".
    • Hands out to the sides: still in the picture.
    • Hands back down: "tracking" again.
  3. Every attempt is the same: start still with hands down, raise, sign once at natural speed, hold half a second, then hands down and still for 1 second. The tutor only answers after your hands are down.
  4. Learning a sign you don't know:
    • Watch the native video at 0.5× with Mirror on.
    • Check the Left and Right side views; they show the palm direction.
    • Compare two or three of the native signers.
    • Describe the sign to yourself in five parts: hands, handshape, where, palm, movement.
    • Sign along slowly, then practise until it's recognised twice out of three tries.

Then three sittings:

Sitting What you do About
1 Learn the 12 test words, then 24 careful attempts in Test mode 45–60 min
2 54 deliberate mistakes: each one changes exactly one thing (sign ~20 cm higher or lower, wrist turned a quarter turn, fist ↔ flat hand, movement a quarter as big, movement reversed, second hand left in your lap) 40–50 min
3 Learn 23 words, then sign 12 short sentences in ISL order with a 1-second rest between signs, and judge whether the English meaning is right 45–60 min

After each test attempt, watch the replay of yourself next to the native signer. Click Yes, log it only if you really did what you intended; otherwise click No, don't count it.

When you're done, open the Results tab, click Refresh, and send me the numbers or a screenshot. Also send any feedback message that looked wrong; the replay helps you judge that. TESTING.md also has a troubleshooting table (camera blocked, no result after signing, flickering hands, slow tracking) and a comparison table to fill in against the native-signer results.
